{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{103.01517pt}{-16.21924pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

By Lemma
2.14
, we know that the natural transformation from the upper route to the lower route from the top-left corner to the bottom-right corner of (
4.14
) evaluated at
\underline{k}_{C^{I}}\in\mathrm{Shv}(C^{I})
gives

\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})=\operatorname{tr}_{C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c}^{(1)})\in H_{0}^{BM}(\Sht_{G,I}/C^{I},\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{G,I}}).

4.4.2.
Special cycle classes as categorical trace

Restricting the natural transformation
\eta_{\mathfrak{c}^{(1)}}
in (
4.14
) to the full-subcategory
\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})\subset\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{G}\times C^{I})
, we get a natural transformation

\eta_{\mathfrak{c}^{(1)},\mathrm{Nilp}}:\int_{\mathcal{P},I,\mathrm{Nilp}}\circ(\Frob\times\mathrm{id})_{!}\circ T_{V^{I}}\to\int_{\mathcal{P},I,\mathrm{Nilp}}.

Since the functor
\int_{\mathcal{P},\mathrm{Nilp}}:\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G})\to\mathrm{Vect}
admits continuous right adjoint, using the natural transformation
\eta_{\mathfrak{c}^{(1)},\mathrm{Nilp}}
and applying the construction Definition
2.2
, we get a natural map

\operatorname{tr}(\eta_{\mathfrak{c}^{(1)},\mathrm{Nilp}}):\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}((\Frob\times\mathrm{id})_{!}\circ T_{V^{I}},\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G}\times C^{I}))\to\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\mathrm{id},\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})).

4.4.3.
Relating geometric trace and categorical trace

The main result in this section is the following:

Theorem 4.9
.

We have a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}((\Frob\times\mathrm{id})_{!}\circ T_{V^{I}}\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{I,!}(\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{G,I}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\mathrm{id},\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{k}_{C^{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta^{(1)}_{\mathfrak{c},\mathrm{Nilp}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{Serre}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Proof.

Using the duality on
\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G})
with unit and counit
(\mathrm{u},\ev)
, the element
\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{\mathfrak{c}^{(1)},\mathrm{Nilp}})
is computed as the composition of natural transformations in the diagram

(4.15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{u}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Frob_{\Bun_{G}}\times\mathrm{id})_{!}\circ T_{V^{I}}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int_{\mathcal{P},I,\mathrm{Nilp}}\otimes\int_{\mathcal{P},I,\mathrm{Nilp},\mathrm{R}}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\mathcal{P},\mathrm{Nilp}}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int_{\mathcal{P},I,\mathrm{Nilp}}\otimes\int_{\mathcal{P},I,\mathrm{Nilp},\mathrm{R}}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathfrak{c}^{(1)},\mathrm{Nilp}}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{\mathcal{P},\mathrm{Nilp}}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

in which the left and right squares are the two back squares in Proposition
4.8
. By Proposition
4.8
, there is a natural map from the diagram (
4.15
) to (
4.14
) (such a map means three commutative cubes), which gives our desired identity.

∎

4.5.
Proof of main result

Proof of Theorem
1.10
and Theorem
1.11
.

Take
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}_{X}
in Theorem
4.9
for the spherical variety
X
in each case.
Theorem
1.10
follows from Theorem
4.9
and Theorem
3.2
. Theorem
1.11
follows from Theorem
4.9
and Theorem
3.3
.
∎

5.
Isotypic part of special cycle classes

In this section, we study the restriction of special cycle classes on the isotypic part of the cohomology of Shtukas.

•

In §
5.1
, we review some basic properties of the geometric isotypic part.

•

In §
5.2
, we study the isotypic part of special cycle classes for split semisimple groups.

•

In §
5.3
, we study the isotypic part of special cycle classes for split reductive groups.

•

In §
5.4
, we study the isotypic part of special cycle classes which have middle-dimension on the generic fiber.

•

In §
5.5
, we study the isotypic part of diagonal cycle classes.

5.1.
Isotypic part of geometric period

In this section, we recall the geometric isotypic part introduced in
[
LW25
]
and its basic properties.

For a
\check{G}
-local system
\sigma\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\check{G}}(k)
and a Hecke eigensheaf
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\in\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G})
, given an affine smooth
G
-variety
X
, the geometric isotypic part of the
X
-period integral is defined to be the complex
\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\in\mathrm{Vect}
, which we simply call the
geometric isotypic part
.

5.1.1.
Hecke action

The geometric isotypic part is equipped with Hecke actions, which we now recall. For
V^{I}\in\Rep(\check{G}^{I})
, given a cohomological correspondence
\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})
, there is an induced natural transformation constructed in §
2.2.6

\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}}}:\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp},I}\circ T_{V^{I}}\to\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp},I}.

Evaluating the natural transformation
\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}}}
at
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}}\in\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})
, we get a map

a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}},\sigma}:V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\to\underline{k}_{C^{I}}\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}.

Since there is an obvious isomorphism

\Hom^{0}(V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma},\underline{k}_{C^{I}}\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})\cong\Hom^{0}(\Gamma(V_{\sigma}^{I})\langle-d_{I}+2r\rangle\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma},\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}),

we can also treat
a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}},\sigma}
as a map

(5.1)

a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}},\sigma}:\Gamma(V_{\sigma}^{I})\langle-d_{I}+2r\rangle\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\to\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}.

We say that the map
a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}},\sigma}
equips the geometric isotypic part
\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
with a Hecke action by
\Gamma(V_{\sigma}^{I})\langle-d_{I}+2r\rangle
. Equivalently, we can write the data above as a map

a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}},\sigma}:\Gamma(V_{\sigma}^{I})\langle-d_{I}+2r\rangle\to\End(\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}).

One can also fix a point
c^{I}\in C^{I}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
and consider the Hecke operator with leg restricted to
c^{I}
which is
T_{V^{I},c^{I}}:\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G})\to\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G})
. In this case, one can restrict the cohomological correspondence
\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}}
to
\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I},c^{I}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{G,c^{I}},\IC_{V^{I}}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}(\mathcal{P}_{X},\mathcal{P}_{X})
. Here, we define
\Hk_{G,c^{I}}:=\Hk_{G,I}\times_{C^{I}}\{c^{I}\}
. It gives a map

(5.2)

a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I},c^{I}},\sigma}:V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\to\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}.

However, the data of (
5.2
) is completely contained in (
5.1
) as follows: Note that there is a map obtained from
(i_{!},i^{!})
-adjunction for
i:\{c^{I}\}\to C^{I}

[c^{I}]:V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\to\Gamma(V_{\sigma}^{I})\langle 2r\rangle.

Then we have
a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I},c^{I}},\sigma}=a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}},\sigma}\circ([c^{I}]\otimes\mathrm{id})
.

5.1.2.
Associativity

The Hecke action (
5.1
) enjoys associativity for composition of cohomological correspondences.

Take
I=\{1,2\cdots,r\}
. Given
V_{i}\in\Rep(\check{G})
and
\mathfrak{c}_{V_{i}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{G,\{i\}},\IC_{V_{i}}\langle-d_{i}\rangle}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C})
for
i\in I
, consider the cohomological correspondence arising from composition

\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}}=\mathfrak{c}_{V_{1}}\circ\mathfrak{c}_{V_{2}}\circ\cdots\circ\mathfrak{c}_{V_{r}}

for
V^{I}=\boxtimes_{i\in I}V_{i}
and
d_{I}=\sum_{i\in I}d_{i}
whose definition is as follows: Note that for each
i\in I
there is a map

\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{G,\{i\}},\IC_{V_{i}}\langle-d_{i}\rangle}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C})\to\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{G,I},\IC_{V_{i}}\langle-d_{i}\rangle}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}}).

Here the sheaf
\IC_{V_{i}}\in\mathrm{Shv}(\Hk_{G,I})
is understood as
\IC_{\mathrm{triv}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes V_{i}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\mathrm{triv}}\in\mathrm{Shv}(\Hk_{G,I})
for
\mathrm{triv}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes V_{i}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\mathrm{triv}\in\Rep(\check{G}^{I})
. Putting this together with the usual composition of cohomological correspondences, we get a map

\begin{split}\circ:\prod_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{G,\{i\}},\IC_{V_{i}}\langle-d_{i}\rangle}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C})&\to\prod_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{G,I},\IC_{V_{i}}\langle-d_{i}\rangle}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\\
&\to\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\end{split}.

In this case, we have

a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}},\sigma}=a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V_{1}},\sigma}\circ\cdots\circ a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V_{r}},\sigma}\in\Hom^{0}(\Gamma(V_{\sigma}^{I})\langle-d_{I}+2r\rangle\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma},\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})

where the later is understood as the composition

\begin{split}a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V_{1}},\sigma}\circ\cdots\circ a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V_{r}},\sigma}:\Gamma(V_{\sigma}^{I})\langle-d_{I}+2r\rangle\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}&=\otimes_{i=1}^{r}\Gamma(V_{i,\sigma})\langle-d_{i}+2\rangle\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\\
&\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}\otimes a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V_{r}}}}\otimes_{i=1}^{r-1}\Gamma(V_{i,\sigma})\langle-d_{i}+2\rangle\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\\
&\cdots\\
&\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}\otimes a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V_{1}}}}\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\end{split}.

5.1.3.
Commutator relation

When the cohomological correspondences
\mathfrak{c}_{V_{i}}
come from local special cohomological correspondences as defined in
[
LW25
, Definition 4.39]
, the Hecke action (
5.1
) enjoys commutator relations coming from the commutator relations in the local Plancherel algebra. By associativity, it is enough to consider the case
I=\{1,2\}
. Assume each
\mathfrak{c}_{V_{i}}
comes from a local special cohomological correspondence
\mathfrak{c}_{V_{i}}^{\mathrm{l}}
. Moreover, assume
[\mathfrak{c}_{V_{1}}^{\mathrm{l}},\mathfrak{c}_{V_{2}}^{\mathrm{l}}]=\hbar\cdot\mathfrak{c}^{\mathrm{l}}_{V_{1}\otimes V_{2}}
for some local special cohomological correspondence
\mathfrak{c}^{\mathrm{l}}_{V_{1}\otimes V_{2}}
. We use

\mathfrak{c}_{V_{1}\otimes V_{2}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{G,\{1\},\IC_{V_{1}\otimes V_{2}}}\langle-d_{1}-d_{2}+2\rangle}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C})

to denote the corresponding global special cohomological correspondence. In this case, assuming
[
LW25
, Conjecture 4.45]
, we have

a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V_{1}}}\circ a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V_{2}}}-a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V_{2}}}\circ a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V_{1}}}=a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V_{1}\otimes V_{2}}}\circ(\cup\otimes\mathrm{id})\in\Hom^{0}(\Gamma(V_{1,\sigma})\otimes\Gamma(V_{2,\sigma})\langle-d_{1}-d_{2}+4\rangle\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma},\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})

where
\cup:\Gamma(V_{1,\sigma})\otimes\Gamma(V_{2,\sigma})\to\Gamma((V_{1}\otimes V_{2})_{\sigma})
is the cup product.

5.2.
Isotypic part of special cycle classes I

From now on, assume that
\sigma\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\check{G}}^{\mathrm{arith}}(k)
is a
\check{G}
-Weil local system and the Hecke eigensheaf
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
is equipped with a compatible Weil sheaf structure. We drop the subscript
V^{I}
in
\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}}
and write
\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}}
.

5.2.1.
Fake special cycle classes

From the Hecke action (
5.1
), one can construct the
fake special cycle class

z_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}\in H^{0}(\Gamma(V_{\sigma}^{I})\langle-d_{I}+2r\rangle)^{*}\cong\Hom^{0}(V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle,\underline{k}_{C^{I}})

defined as the element satisfying

(5.3)

z_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}(m)=\operatorname{tr}(a_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}(m)\circ\Frob,\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})

for any
m\in H^{0}(\Gamma(V_{\sigma}^{I})\langle-d_{I}+2r\rangle)
. See (
1.27
) for an alternative but equivalent definition. Here, we are assuming the trace written above is convergent. The ideal case is the following:

Assumption 5.1
.

The complex
\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
is perfect.

We will assume Assumption
5.1
until the end of §
5.2
. Under this assumption, the fake special cycle classes admit the following interpretation via categorical trace: Consider the lax-commutative square

(5.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{a_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

in which the natural transformation is defined as

\eta_{a_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}}:-\otimes V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\xrightarrow{-\otimes a_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}}-\otimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}}\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}.

The natural transformation
\eta_{a_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}}
admits a Frobenius twist

\eta_{a_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}}^{(1)}:-\otimes V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\xrightarrow{-\otimes a_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}\circ(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Frob)}-\otimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}}\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}.

Then one has a commutative diagram

(5.5)

\hbox to255.15pt{\vbox to58.94pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 127.57407pt\lower-29.47176pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

Here, the horizontal maps are the most obvious isomorphisms. The left vertical map is induced by the functoriality of categorical trace in Definition
2.2
.

5.2.2.
Fake versus real I

For a cohomological correspondence

\mathfrak{c}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})

and
\sigma\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\check{G}}^{\mathrm{arith}}(k)
. On the one hand, one has the fake special cycle classes

z_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}\in\Hom^{0}(V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle,\underline{k}_{C^{I}})

whenever it is well-defined. On the other hand, one has the geometric trace

\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})\in\Hom^{0}(l_{I,!}(\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{G,I}})\langle-d_{I}\rangle,\underline{k}_{C^{I}}).

We would like to relate these two.

The most natural way of doing this is to construct a map

(5.6)

\xi_{\sigma,I}:V_{\sigma}^{I}\to l_{I,!}(\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{G,I}})

which only depends on the Hecke eigensheaf
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
(in particular, independent of the
G
-variety
X
) and ask if

(5.7)

z_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}=\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})\circ\xi_{\sigma,I}.

It turns out that one can achieve this under good assumptions.

In this subsection, we will achieve this under a stronger assumption compared to Assumption
5.1
, which is:

Assumption 5.2
.

The group
G
is split semisimple, and the object
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\in\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G})
is compact.

Remark 5.3
.

Note that since the functor
\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}:\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G})\to\mathrm{Vect}
preserves compact objects, Assumption
5.1
is a consequence of Assumption
5.2
.

Remark 5.4
.

By
[
GR25
]
, Assumption
5.2
is true when
G
is semisimple and
\sigma\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\check{G}}(k)
is (geometrically) irreducible. Unfortunately, in our application §
6
, we are interested in the case
G=\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n-1}
, which is not semisimple. We will loosen Assumption
5.2
in §
5.3
.

Until the end of §
5.2.2
, we assume Assumption
5.2
. In this case, there is a natural choice of
\xi_{\sigma,I}
which we are going to explain now.

Note that the diagram (
5.4
) naturally factors as

(5.8)

Here, the upper commutative diagram can be taken as the definition of the map
\xi_{\sigma,I}
. The lower commutative diagram is Theorem
4.9
. By (
5.9
), we know that the outer square of (
5.10
) is the square in (
5.5
). This gives the desired identity
z_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}=\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})\circ\xi_{\sigma,I}
.

5.2.3.
Refinement

Note that
l_{I,!}(\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{G,I}})=0
unless the central characters of different components of
V^{I}\in\Rep(\check{G}^{I})
add up to zero, which we assume in this section. In this case, all the constructions above make sense after adding a superscript
e\in\pi_{0}(\Bun_{G})\cong\pi_{1}(G)
, which means restricting to the connected component
\Bun_{G}^{e}\subset\Bun_{G}
. This gives us refined notions including Hecke eigensheaf on the connected component
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}=\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}|_{\Bun_{G}^{e}}
, cohomological correspondence

\mathfrak{c}^{e}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{G,I}^{e},\IC_{V^{I}}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}}),

Hecke action on the geometric isotypic part

a^{e}_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}=a_{\mathfrak{c}^{e},\sigma}:=V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}\to\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e},

isotypic part map

(5.11)

\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}:V_{\sigma}^{I}\to l_{I,!}(\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{G,I}^{e}}),

the fake special cycle classes

(5.12)

z^{e}_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}:V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle\to\underline{k}_{C^{I}}

constructed from
a_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}^{e}
, and the identity

(5.13)

z^{e}_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}=\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})\circ\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}.

5.3.
Isotypic part of special cycle classes II

In this section, we will generalize the technique in §
5.2.2
to general split reductive groups
G
. Let
Z(G)
be the center of
G
and
S=G/[G,G]
. We have a natural map on the Langlands dual groups
\check{S}\to\check{G}
. Define
\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S})\subset\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{S})
to be the direct summand containing the constant sheaf
\underline{k}_{\Bun_{S}}
. We have an obvious map
f:\Bun_{G}\to\Bun_{S}
.

The main difference between this section and §
5.2.2
is that we replace Assumption
5.2
by the following which we assume throughout §
5.3
:

Assumption 5.5
.

The functor
f^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}:\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S})\to\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G})
preserves compact objects.

Remark 5.6
.

By
[
GR25
]
, Assumption
5.5
is true whenever
\sigma\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\check{G}}(k)
is (geometrically) irreducible.

5.3.1.
Construction of the isotypic part

In this section, we are going to construct the map (
5.6
) for
V^{I}\in\Rep(\check{G}^{I})
and
\sigma\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\check{G}}^{\mathrm{arith}}(k)
, which only depends on the choice of a Hecke eigensheaf
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\in\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G})
with a Weil sheaf structure compatible with that of
\sigma
.

Consider the functor
(-)_{\sigma}:\Rep(\check{G}^{I})\to\mathrm{Shv}(\Hk_{S,I})
defined by
V^{I}\mapsto V^{I}_{\sigma}
. We explain the notation as follows: Note that
\Hk_{S,I}=\coprod_{\lambda_{S,I}\in X_{*}(S)^{I}}\Hk_{S,\lambda_{S,I}}
. When
V^{I}
admits a unique central character
\lambda_{S,I}\in X^{*}(\check{S})^{I}
, we abuse the notation and denote
V^{I}_{\sigma}=l_{S,I}^{*}V^{I}_{\sigma}|_{\Hk_{S,\lambda_{S,I}}}
in which
l_{S,I}:\Hk_{S,I}\to C^{I}
is the map remembering only the legs. In the expression, the later
V^{I}_{\sigma}
is the local system
V_{\sigma}^{I}\in\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})
while the former
V_{\sigma}^{I}
is a sheaf
V_{\sigma}^{I}\in\mathrm{Shv}(\Hk_{S,\lambda_{S,I}})\subset\mathrm{Shv}(\Hk_{S,I})
. The meaning of
V_{\sigma}^{I}
will be clear from the context. In general,
V^{I}
is a direct sum of representations with different central characters. We take the direct sum of the construction above for each direct summand of
V^{I}
.

When
G
is split reductive but not necessarily semisimple, it is necessary to make the refinement in §
5.2.3
. We will fix
e\in\pi_{0}(\Bun_{G})
until the end of this section. We use
\overline{e}\in\pi_{0}(\Bun_{S})
to denote the image of
e
under the map
\pi_{0}(\Bun_{G})\to\pi_{0}(\Bun_{S})
. From now on, without loss of generality, we always assume
V^{I}\in\Rep(\check{G}^{I})
is irreducible with highest weight
\lambda_{I}
and central character
\lambda_{S,I}=(\lambda_{S,1},\cdots,\lambda_{S,r})\in X^{*}(S)^{I}
. Moreover, we can assume

(5.14)

\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{S,i}=0

since otherwise
\Sht_{G,\lambda_{I}}=\varnothing
.

Consider the correspondence

Define

T_{V^{I}_{\sigma}}:\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{S}\times C^{I})\to\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{S}\times C^{I})

by

T_{V^{I}_{\sigma}}(-):=\overrightarrow{h}_{S,I,!}(\overleftarrow{h}_{S,I}^{*}(-)\otimes V^{I}_{\sigma})

where
V_{\sigma}^{I}\in\mathrm{Shv}(\Hk_{G,\lambda_{S,I}})
. This functor preserves the subcategory
\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})
and induces a functor

T_{V^{I}_{\sigma}}:\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})\to\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I}).

We have a natural transformation

(5.15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{V_{\sigma}^{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{V^{I}}}

To define the natural transformation
\eta_{\sigma}
, we consider the diagram

(5.16)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{G}\times C^{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hk_{G,\lambda_{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{G}\times C^{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{S}\times C^{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hk_{S,\lambda_{S,I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{S}\times C^{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\Hk}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overleftarrow{h}_{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{h}_{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overleftarrow{h}_{S,I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{h}_{S,I}}

in which
\overleftarrow{h}_{S,I}
and
\overrightarrow{h}_{S,I}
are isomorphisms, the natural transformation
\eta_{\sigma}
is defined as

\begin{split}((f^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{id})\circ T_{V^{I}_{\sigma}}&=(f\times\mathrm{id})^{*}(\overrightarrow{h}_{S,I,!}(\overleftarrow{h}_{S,I}^{*}(-)\otimes V^{I}_{\sigma}))\otimes(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\\
&\cong(f\times\mathrm{id})^{*}(\overrightarrow{h}_{S,I,!}\overleftarrow{h}_{S,I}^{*}(-))\otimes(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\boxtimes V_{\sigma}^{I})\\
&\cong(f\times\mathrm{id})^{*}(\overrightarrow{h}_{S,I,!}\overleftarrow{h}_{S,I}^{*}(-))\otimes\overrightarrow{h}_{I,!}(\overleftarrow{h}_{I}^{*}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\otimes\IC_{V^{I}})\\
&\cong\overrightarrow{h}_{I,!}(\overrightarrow{h}_{I}^{*}(f\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\overrightarrow{h}_{S,I,!}\overleftarrow{h}_{S,I}^{*}(-)\otimes\overleftarrow{h}_{I}^{*}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\otimes\IC_{V^{I}})\\
&\cong\overrightarrow{h}_{I,!}(f_{\Hk}^{*}\overrightarrow{h}_{S,I}^{*}\overrightarrow{h}_{S,I,!}\overleftarrow{h}_{S,I}^{*}(-)\otimes\overleftarrow{h}_{I}^{*}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\otimes\IC_{V^{I}})\\
&\cong\overrightarrow{h}_{I,!}(f_{\Hk}^{*}\overleftarrow{h}_{S,I}^{*}(-)\otimes\overleftarrow{h}_{I}^{*}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\otimes\IC_{V^{I}})\\
&\cong\overrightarrow{h}_{I,!}(\overleftarrow{h}_{I}^{*}((f\times\mathrm{id})^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\otimes\IC_{V^{I}})\\
&=T_{V^{I}}\circ((f^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{id})\end{split}.

Adding a Frobenius twist, we get a natural transformation

\eta_{\sigma}^{(1)}:((f^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{id})\circ(\Frob\times\mathrm{id})_{!}\circ T_{V^{I}_{\sigma}}\to(\Frob\times\mathrm{id})_{!}\circ T_{V^{I}}\circ((f^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{id}).

5.15
) preserve compact objects by Assumption
5.5
, by Definition
2.2
, we get the left vertical map of the following commutative diagram

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Here, the map
\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}
is defined such that the diagram above is commutative. We need to explain the isomorphism

(5.18)

\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}:\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}((\Frob\times\mathrm{id})_{!}\circ T_{V^{I}_{\sigma}},\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}V_{\sigma}^{I}.

The inclusion

\iota_{S,\mathrm{triv}}:\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}})\to\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}})

admits a continuous right adjoint

\iota_{S,\mathrm{triv},\mathrm{R}}:\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}})\to\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}}),

which is the composition of the Beilinson’s spectral projector
\iota_{S,\mathrm{R}}:\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}})\to\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}})
and the projection to the direct summand
\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}})\subset\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}})
. The category
\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}})
is self-dual under the Verdier duality with unit

\mathrm{u}_{S}=\iota_{S,\mathrm{triv},\mathrm{R}}(\Delta_{S,*}\omega_{\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}}})\in\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}})^{\otimes 2}

and counit

\ev_{S}=\Gamma_{\blacktriangle}\circ\Delta_{S}^{!}:\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}})^{\otimes 2}\to\mathrm{Vect}.

Here
\Delta_{S}:\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}}\to\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}}\times\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}}
is the diagonal map, and
\Gamma_{\blacktriangle}:\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}})\to\mathrm{Vect}
is the unique continuous functor whose restriction to compact objects is
\Gamma
. This is a particular case of the renormalized functor of direct image, which is denoted
f_{\blacktriangle}
in
[
AGK+22c
, §A.2.3]
.

The map
\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}
is defined as

(5.19)

\begin{split}\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}:&\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}((\Frob\times\mathrm{id})_{!}\circ T_{V^{I}_{\sigma}},\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I}))\\
\cong&\pr_{2,\blacktriangle}(\Delta_{S}\times\mathrm{id})^{!}(\Frob_{\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}}}\times\mathrm{id})_{*}\circ T_{(V^{I}\boxtimes\mathrm{triv})_{\sigma}}(\iota_{S,\mathrm{triv}}\iota_{S,\mathrm{triv},\mathrm{R}}\Delta_{S,*}\omega_{\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}}}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\\
\to&\pr_{2,\blacktriangle}(\Delta_{S}\times\mathrm{id})^{!}(\Frob_{\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}}}\times\mathrm{id})_{*}\circ T_{(V^{I}\boxtimes\mathrm{triv})_{\sigma}}(\Delta_{S,*}\omega_{\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}}}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\\
\cong&l_{S,I,*}(V_{\sigma}^{I}\otimes\omega_{\Sht_{S,\lambda_{S,I}}^{\overline{e}}})\\
\cong&l_{S,I,*}(V_{\sigma}^{I}|_{\Sht_{S,\lambda_{S,I}}^{\overline{e}}})\end{split}.

Here,
\pr_{2}:\Bun_{S}\times C^{I}\to C^{I}
is the projection to the second coordinate, the second map uses the adjunction
\iota_{S,\mathrm{triv}}\iota_{S,\mathrm{triv},\mathrm{R}}\to\mathrm{id}
, the map
l_{S,I}:\Sht_{S,I}\to C^{I}
is the map remembering only the legs. The map
\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}
is a special case of the refined true local term map defined in
[
GV24
, §4.11]
in which the same map is denoted by
\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}_{c,\blacktriangle}
.

Note that there is a natural map
V_{\sigma}^{I}\to l_{S,I,*}(V_{\sigma}^{I}|_{\Sht_{S,\lambda_{S,I}}^{\overline{e}}})
induced by
(l_{S,I}^{*},l_{S,I,*})
-adjunction, which realizes
V_{\sigma}^{I}
as a direct summand of the later. One easily checks that the map (
5.19
) factors through
V_{\sigma}^{I}
and defines the isomorphism (
5.18
). This explains the construction of the
\sigma
-isotypic part
\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}:V_{\sigma}^{I}\to l_{I,!}(\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{G,I}^{e}})
.

The
\sigma
-isotypic part map
\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}
admits a less canonical but equivalent and simpler construction, which is more convenient to use. For simplicity in explanation, we assume that the curve
C
admits a rational point
c\in C(\mathbb{F}_{q})
.
13
13

13

In general, one can choose a finite subscheme of
C
and run a parallel argument.
In this case, instead of considering the map
f:\Bun_{G}\to\Bun_{S}
, one chooses a point
c\in C(\mathbb{F}_{q})
and consider the map
f_{c}=(-)_{c}\circ f:\Bun_{G}\to[*/S]
where
(-)_{c}:\Bun_{S}\to[*/S]
is taking stalk at
c\in C
. In this case, one replaces the diagram (
5.16
) by

(5.20)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{G}\times C^{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hk_{G,\lambda_{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{G}\times C^{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[*/S]\times C^{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[*/S]\times C^{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[*/S]\times C^{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{c}\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{c,\Hk}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overleftarrow{h}_{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{h}_{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{c}\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{\lambda_{S,I}}}

where
t_{\lambda_{S,I}}(\mathcal{E}_{0},c^{I})=(\mathcal{E}_{0}\otimes\mathcal{O}(-\lambda_{S,I}\cdot c^{I})_{c},c^{I})\in[*/S]\times C^{I}
. Define

T_{c,V_{\sigma}^{I}}=t_{\lambda_{S,I},!}(-\otimes V_{\sigma}^{I}):\mathrm{Shv}([*/S])\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})\to\mathrm{Shv}([*/S])\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I}).

In this case, the diagram (
5.15
) can be composed with the upper square in the following diagram, and we arrive at

(5.21)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}([*/S])\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}([*/S])\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G}^{e})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G}^{e})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{c}^{*}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{c,V_{\sigma}^{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{c}^{*}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{c,S,\sigma}^{\overline{e}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{V_{\sigma}^{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{V^{I}}}

Here, the natural transformation
\eta_{c,S,\sigma}^{\overline{e}}
is the obvious one. We denote
\eta_{c,\sigma}^{e}=\eta_{\sigma}\circ\eta_{c,S,\sigma}^{\overline{e}}
. Since all the vertical maps in (
5.21
) preserves compact objects, by the formalism of Definition
2.2
, we arrive at a commutative square

(5.22)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}((\Frob\times\mathrm{id})_{!}\circ T_{c,V_{\sigma}^{I}},\mathrm{Shv}([*/S])\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\sigma}^{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}((\Frob\times\mathrm{id})_{!}\circ T_{V^{I}_{\sigma}},\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Bun_{S}^{\overline{e}})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\sigma}^{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}((\Frob\times\mathrm{id})_{!}\circ T_{V^{I}},\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G}^{e})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{I,!}(\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{G,I}^{e}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{c,S,\sigma}^{\overline{e}(1)})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{\sigma}^{(1)})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{Serre}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Here the definition of the top horizontal isomorphism
\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}
is similar to but simpler than (
5.18
). The commutativity of the upper square follows from a routine generalization of
[
GV24
, Theorem 0.4(b)(i)]
to cohomological correspondences with kernel. Therefore, the outer square of (
5.22
) gives a simpler definition of the map
\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}:V_{\sigma}^{I}\to l_{I,!}(\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{G,I}^{e}})
.

5.3.2.
Restriction of special cycle classes to the isotypic part

Keep the same assumptions as in the previous section. Suppose we are given an affine smooth
G
-variety
X
and a cohomological correspondence

\mathfrak{c}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}}),

we can consider the restriction of the geometric Shtuka construction of special cohomological correspondence
\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c}):l_{I,!}(\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{G,I}}\langle-d_{I}\rangle)\to\underline{k}_{C^{I}}
along the isotypic part map
\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}:V_{\sigma}^{I}\to l_{I,!}(\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{G,I}^{e}})
, which is the composition

\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})\circ\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}:V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle\to\underline{k}_{C^{I}}.

Since all the vertical maps in the diagram above preserve compact objects, applying the formalism in Definition
2.2
, we arrive at the left column of the commutative diagram

(5.24)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}((\Frob\times\mathrm{id})_{!}\circ T_{c,V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle},\mathrm{Shv}([*/S])\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}((\Frob\times\mathrm{id})_{!}\circ T_{V^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle},\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G}^{e})\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{I,!}(\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{G,I}^{e}}\langle-d_{I}\rangle)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\mathrm{id},\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{k}_{C^{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{c,\sigma}^{e(1)})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{Serre}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{\mathfrak{c},\mathrm{Nilp}}^{(1)})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Define

(5.25)

\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{e}}:=\eta_{\mathfrak{c},\mathrm{Nilp}}\circ\eta_{c,\sigma}^{e}.

This gives the identity

(5.26)

\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})\circ\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}=\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{e}}^{(1)}).

We give another description of the natural transformation
\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{e}}
. Note that

\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp},I}\circ((f_{c}^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e})\otimes\mathrm{id})\cong\Gamma_{c}(-\otimes f_{c,!}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}))\otimes\mathrm{id}.

We claim that the natural transformation
\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{e}}
comes from a cohomological correspondence
\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{e}
via the formalism in §
2.2.6
where

(5.27)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{e}&\in\mathrm{Corr}_{[*/S]\times C^{I},V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}(f_{c,!}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e})\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}},f_{c,!}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e})\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\\
&=\Hom^{0}(V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle\otimes t_{\lambda_{S,I}}^{*}(f_{c,!}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e})\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}}),f_{c,!}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e})\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\end{split}

constructed as:

(5.28)

\begin{split}V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle\otimes t_{\lambda_{S,I}}^{*}(f_{c,!}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e})\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})&\cong t_{\lambda_{S,I}}^{*}(f_{c}\times\mathrm{id})_{!}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\otimes\overrightarrow{h}_{I,!}(\overleftarrow{h}_{I}^{*}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\otimes\IC_{V^{I}}\langle-d_{I}\rangle))\\
&\cong t_{\lambda_{S,I}}^{*}(f_{c}\times\mathrm{id})_{!}\overrightarrow{h}_{I,!}(\overrightarrow{h}_{I}^{*}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\otimes\overleftarrow{h}_{I}^{*}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\otimes\IC_{V^{I}}\langle-d_{I}\rangle)\\
&\cong(f_{c}\times\mathrm{id})_{!}\overleftarrow{h}_{I,!}(\overrightarrow{h}_{I}^{*}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\otimes\overleftarrow{h}_{I}^{*}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\otimes\IC_{V^{I}}\langle-d_{I}\rangle)\\
&\cong(f_{c}\times\mathrm{id})_{!}(\overleftarrow{h}_{I,!}(\overrightarrow{h}_{I}^{*}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\otimes\IC_{V^{I}}\langle-d_{I}\rangle)\otimes(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}}))\\
&\to f_{c,!}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}}\end{split}

in which the last step uses the cohomological correspondence
\mathfrak{c}:\overleftarrow{h}_{I,!}(\overrightarrow{h}_{I}^{*}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\otimes\IC_{V^{I}}\langle-d_{I}\rangle)\to\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}}
.

5.3.3.
Fake versus real II

In this section, we would like to generalize the identity (
5.13
). We work under the following assumption, which is weaker than Assumption
5.1
:

Assumption 5.7
.

The complex
\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}
is perfect.

In this case, we have the fake special cycle classes

z_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}^{e}:V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle\to\underline{k}_{C^{I}},

the special cycle classes

\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c}):l_{I,!}(\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{G,I}}\langle-d_{I}\rangle)\to\underline{k}_{C^{I}},

and the isotypic part map

\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}:V_{\sigma}^{I}\to l_{I,!}(\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{G,I}^{e}}).

The main result in this subsection is the following:

Proposition 5.8
.

Assuming Assumption
5.7
and Assumption
5.5
, we have
z_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}^{e}=\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})\circ\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}
.

Proof of Proposition
5.8
.

The basic idea is to replace the axillary category
\mathrm{Shv}([*/S])
by
\mathrm{Vect}
, and we will get back to the situation in §
5.2.2
. As a naive tempt, we consider the diagram

(5.29)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}([*/S])\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}([*/S])\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{c}^{*}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{c}^{*}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{p_{c}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{c,V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\circ(f_{c}^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}))\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\circ(f_{c}^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}))\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{e}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

Where we use the map
p_{c}:[*/S]\to*
. Here, the lower square is the outer square of (
5.23
), the natural transformation in the upper square is the obvious one.

p_{c}^{*}
preserves compact objects, one would hope for a commutative diagram

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Since
\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{e}}\circ\eta_{p_{c}}=\eta_{a_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}^{e}}
, we get the desired identity in Proposition
5.8
. However, the functor
p_{c}^{*}
does not preserve compact objects. We cannot directly apply the formalism in Definition
2.2
.

We bypass this point by considering the category
\mathrm{Shv}([*/S])^{\mathrm{ren}}
, which is the renormalization of
\mathrm{Shv}([*/S])
such that constructible complexes are compact. There is a fully faithful embedding
\mathrm{ren}:\mathrm{Shv}([*/S])\to\mathrm{Shv}([*/S]^{\mathrm{ren}})
preserving compact objects. We refer to
[
AGK+22c
, §F.5]
for a thorough introduction to this renormalized category.

One can bypass the problem by considering the diagram

(5.31)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}(*/S)^{\mathrm{ren}}\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}(*/S)^{\mathrm{ren}}\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}(*/S)\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}(*/S)\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{c}^{*}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{c}^{*}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{p_{c}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{c,V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{c,V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\circ(f_{c}^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}))\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ren}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\circ(f_{c}^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}))\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ren}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathrm{ren}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{e}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

in which all the natural transformations are the obvious ones. In this diagram, all vertical arrows preserve compact objects. We can apply the formalism in Definition
2.2
to obtain the diagram

(5.32)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(-\otimes V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle,\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}((\Frob\times\mathrm{id})_{!}\circ T_{c,V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle},\mathrm{Shv}(*/S)^{\mathrm{ren}}\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}((\Frob\times\mathrm{id})_{!}\circ T_{c,V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle},\mathrm{Shv}(*/S)\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\mathrm{id},\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{k}_{C^{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{p_{c}}^{(1)})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{\mathrm{ren}}^{(1)})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{e}}^{(1)})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})\circ\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Here, the commutativity of the upper square follows from a direct generalization of
[
GV24
, Theorem 0.4(b)(i)]
to cohomological correspondences with kernel. The commutativity of the middle square follows from the same kind of generalization of
[
GV24
, Proposition 4.12]
. The bottom square is the outer square of (
5.24
).

By the commutativity of (
5.32
), we are reduced to show

(5.33)

\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{e}}^{(1)})\circ\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{\mathrm{ren}}^{(1)})^{-1}\circ\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{p_{c}}^{(1)})=z_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}^{e}.

Moreover, each category in the diagram is equipped with an endomorphism which has been specified in (
5.31
), among which the only new natural transformation is

(5.35)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}(*/S)^{\mathrm{ren}}\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(c^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Shv}(*/S)^{\mathrm{ren}}\otimes\mathrm{QLisse}(c^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{c,V_{\sigma}^{I}\langle-d_{I}\rangle}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\circ(f_{c}^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}))\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\circ(f_{c}^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}))\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{e},\mathrm{ren}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

such that
\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{e},\mathrm{ren}}\circ\eta_{\mathrm{ren}}=\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{e}}
.

By Assumption
5.7
, we know
(\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\circ f_{c}^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e})(\underline{k}_{[*/S]})\in\mathrm{Vect}
is compact. Since
\underline{k}_{[*/S]}\in\mathrm{Shv}([*/S])^{\mathrm{ren}}
is a compact generator, we know all maps in (
5.34
) preserve compact objects. Therefore, it gives a commutative diagram

}}}}}.

Since we have
\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{e},\mathrm{ren}}\circ\eta_{p_{c}}=\eta_{a_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}^{e}}
, we know

\begin{split}&\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{e}}^{(1)})\circ\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{\mathrm{ren}}^{(1)})^{-1}\circ\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{p_{c}}^{(1)})\\
=&\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}^{e},\mathrm{ren}}^{(1)})\circ\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{p_{c}}^{(1)})\\
=&\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{QLisse}(C^{I})}(\eta_{a_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}^{e}}^{(1)})\\
=&z_{\mathfrak{c},\sigma}^{e}\end{split}.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.8
.

∎

Remark 5.9
.

Suppose one replaces
\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}
by
\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{Serre}}
for all the horizontal maps in (
5.32
), we do not know how to prove the commutativity of the top square.
14
14

14

It might be possible to generalize the argument in
[
AGK+22a
, §6]
and show that
\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}=\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{Serre}}
.
This is the reason that we use
\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}
instead of
\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{Serre}}
even though the latter seems more natural in our setting.

5.4.
Generically middle-dimensional case

In this section, we study the case
d_{I}=0
and
V^{I}\in\Rep(\check{G}^{I})^{\heartsuit}
, which means that the cohomological correspondence is
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})
. In this case, the geometric trace is
\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c}):l_{I,!}(\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{G,I}})\to\underline{k}_{C^{I}}
. We develop a tool to study its restriction to the isotypic part
\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})\circ\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}:V_{\sigma}^{I}\to k_{C^{I}}
. In this section, we assume
V^{I}\in\Rep(\check{G}^{I})^{\heartsuit}
is irreducible with highest weight
\lambda_{I}
whose central character
\lambda_{S,I}\in X_{*}(S)^{I}
satisfies
\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{S,i}=0
.

In this case, taking stalk at
c^{I}\in C^{I}
induces an injection

(-)_{c^{I}}:\Hom^{0}(V_{\sigma}^{I},\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\to\Hom^{0}(V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I},k).

Therefore, we only need to understand the induced map on the stalk
(\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})\circ\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e})_{c^{I}}:V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\to k
.

Note that there is another natural map

\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{e}):V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\to k.

We now explain this map. Consider the restriction along
c^{I}\to C^{I}
of the cohomological correspondence (
5.27
) which is

\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{e}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{[*/S],V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}}(f_{c,!}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e})\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}},f_{c,!}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e})\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}}).

Since
\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{S,i}=0
, we know that the underlying correspondence of
\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{e}
is the identity correspondence on
[*/S]
. The geometric trace above is the map

\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{e}):V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\cong\Fun([*/S(\mathbb{F}_{q})])\otimes V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\cong\Gamma_{c}(\underline{k}_{[*/S(\mathbb{F}_{q})]})\otimes V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\to k

in which we are using the identification
\Fun([*/S(\mathbb{F}_{q})])\cong k
given by evaluation at the unique point.
15
15

15

Note that this isomorphism is
|S(\mathbb{F}_{q})|
times the natural adjunction map
\Gamma_{c}(\underline{k}_{[*/S(\mathbb{F}_{q})]})\to k
.
Note that the trace construction makes sense since
f_{c,!}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e})
is constructible by Assumption
5.5
.

We have the following proposition:

Proposition 5.10
.

We have
(\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})\circ\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e})_{c^{I}}=\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{e})\in\Hom^{0}(V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I},k)
.

Proof of Proposition
5.10
.
