in which that the local term map
\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{Serre}}
is defined by

\begin{split}\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{Serre}}:\operatorname{tr}(\Frob_{!}\circ(-\otimes V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}),\mathrm{Shv}([*/S]))&\cong\Gamma_{c}(\Delta_{c}^{*}(\Frob\times\mathrm{id})_{!}(\Delta_{c,!}\underline{k}_{[*/S]}\otimes V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}))\\
&\cong\Gamma_{c}(\underline{k}_{[*/S(\mathbb{F}_{q})]})\otimes V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\\
&\cong\Fun([*/S(\mathbb{F}_{q})])\otimes V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\\
&\cong V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\end{split}.

Here we use the map
\Delta_{c}:[*/S]\to[*/S]\times[*/S]
, and we use the isomorphism
\Fun([*/S(\mathbb{F}_{q})])\cong k
given by evaluation at the unique point.

On the other hand, by restricting the outer square of (
5.24
) to
c^{I}
, we have a commutative diagram

(5.38)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}(\Frob_{!}\circ(-\otimes V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}),\mathrm{Shv}([*/S]))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}(\mathrm{id},\mathrm{Vect})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}(\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{e}}^{(1)})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})\circ\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e})_{c^{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

in which the local term map
\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}
is defined by

\begin{split}\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}:\operatorname{tr}(\Frob_{!}\circ(-\otimes V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}),\mathrm{Shv}([*/S]))&\cong\Gamma_{\blacktriangle}(\Delta_{c}^{!}(\Frob\times\mathrm{id})_{*}(\Delta_{c,*}\omega_{[*/S]}\otimes V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}))\\
&\cong\Gamma(\omega_{[*/S(\mathbb{F}_{q})]})\otimes V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\\
&\cong\Fun([*/S(\mathbb{F}_{q})])\otimes V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\\
&\cong V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\end{split}.

In which we are also using the isomorphism
\Fun([*/S(\mathbb{F}_{q})])\cong k
given by evaluation at the unique point for the last step. Indeed, the inverse of this map is the adjunction map
k\to\Gamma(\omega_{[*/S(\mathbb{F}_{q})]})
.

By
[
AGK+22a
, Theorem 6.1.4]
, we know
\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{true}}=\mathrm{LT}^{\mathrm{Serre}}
.
16
16

16

Strictly speaking, there is no tensor product
-\otimes V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}
involved in
[
AGK+22a
, Theorem 6.1.4]
. However, it is easy to see that this factor is innocuous and goes through the proof in
loc.cit
.
Combining this with (
5.37
)(
5.38
), we obtain

(\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})\circ\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e})_{c^{I}}=\operatorname{tr}(\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{e}}^{(1)})=\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{e}).

∎

5.5.
Diagonal cycles

In this section, we study the restriction of the diagonal cycle on the isotypic part.

5.5.1.
Conjectural description

We take
G=H\times H
,
X=H\backslash H\times H
, and
\sigma=(\sigma_{H},c^{*}\sigma_{H})\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\check{H}}^{\mathrm{arith}}(k)\times\mathrm{Loc}_{\check{H}}^{\mathrm{arith}}(k)
where
c:\check{H}\to\check{H}
is the Cartan involution (same letter for the induced map
c:\mathrm{Loc}_{\check{H}}\to\mathrm{Loc}_{\check{H}}
). The
\check{H}
-local system
c^{*}\sigma_{H}
is characterized by
V^{*}_{H,c^{*}\sigma}\cong V_{H,\sigma}
for any irreducible
V_{H}\in\Rep(\check{H})
.
17
17

17

Strictly speaking, we have
V_{H,c^{*}\sigma}\cong c^{*}V_{H,\sigma}
. However, one has the natural isomorphism
c^{*}V_{H}\cong V_{H}^{*}
given by the Geometric Satake equivalence.
In this case, we take a Hecke eigensheaf
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}\in\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{H})
for
\sigma_{H}
. One can consider
\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}})\in\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{H})
which is naturally a Hecke eigensheaf with eigenvalue
c^{*}\sigma_{H}
. Here,
\mathbb{D}:\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{G})_{c}\to\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{G})_{c}^{\mathrm{op}}
is the Verdier duality. We take

\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}=\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}\boxtimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}})\in\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{G}).

Fix
e\in\pi_{1}(H)\cong\pi_{0}(\Bun_{H})
. We use
\Bun_{H}^{e}\subset\Bun_{H}
and
\Bun_{G}^{e}\subset\Bun_{G}
to denote the corresponding connected component. Define
S_{H}=H/[H,H]
, then
S=S_{H}\times S_{H}
. Consider irreducible representation
V_{H}\in\Rep(\check{H}^{I})
with highest weight
\lambda_{H,I}
. Take
V^{I}=V_{H}^{I}\boxtimes V_{H}^{I}\in\Rep(\check{G}^{I})
. Assume
V_{H}^{I}
has central character
\lambda_{S_{H},I}=(\lambda_{S_{H},1},\cdots,\lambda_{S_{H},r})\in X^{*}(S_{H})^{I}
and
\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{S_{H},i}=0
.

Define
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}=\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}|_{\Bun_{H}^{e}}
and
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}=\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}|_{\Bun_{G}^{e}}
. We have an isotypic part map

\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}=\xi_{\sigma_{H},I}^{e}\otimes\xi_{c^{*}\sigma_{H},I}^{e}:V_{H,\sigma_{H}}^{I}\otimes V_{H,\sigma_{H}}^{I*}\cong V_{\sigma}^{I}\to l_{I,!}(\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{G,I}^{e}})\cong l_{H,I,!}(\IC_{V_{H}^{I}}|_{\Sht_{H,I}^{e}})\otimes l_{H,I,!}(\IC_{V_{H}^{I}}|_{\Sht_{H,I}^{e}}).

Take the diagonal cohomological correspondence

\mathfrak{c}=\Delta_{\Hk,I,!}[\Hk_{H,\lambda_{H,I}}/\Bun_{H}\times C^{I}]\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})

as in Theorem
3.3
. We would like to understand the restriction of intersection pairing on the isotypic part

\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})\circ\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}:V_{\sigma}^{I}\to\underline{k}_{C^{I}}.

Note that there is the natural evaluation map
\ev_{V_{H,\sigma_{H}}^{I}}:V_{\sigma}^{I}\cong V_{H,\sigma_{H}}^{I}\otimes V_{H,\sigma_{H}}^{I*}\to\underline{k}_{C^{I}}
. It is natural to ask about the relation between these two maps. The following is a conjectural answer:

Conjecture 5.11
.

We have
\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})\circ\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}=\operatorname{tr}(\Frob,\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e})))\cdot\ev_{V_{H,\sigma_{H}}^{I}}
.

We need to explain the meaning of the number
\operatorname{tr}(\Frob,\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e})))
. When
H
is semisimple, assuming Assumption
5.2
, the complex
\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}))
is perfect, hence, the trace is a well-defined number. When
H
is not semisimple, the vector space
\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}))
is usually infinite-dimensional. In this case, we assume Assumption
5.5
, and the sum
\operatorname{tr}(\Frob,\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e})))
will be convergent. In fact, consider the map
f_{c,H}:\Bun_{H}\to[*/S_{H}]
and the diagonal map
\Delta_{c}:[*/S_{H}]\to[*/S]
. Since the functor

\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\circ(f_{c}^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})\cong\Gamma_{c}(-\otimes\Delta_{c,!}f_{c,H,!}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}))):\mathrm{Shv}([*/S])\to\mathrm{Vect}

preserves compact objects, we know
f_{c,H,!}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}))\in\mathrm{Shv}([*/S_{H}])
is constructible. Note that

f_{c,H,!}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}))

is a constant sheaf. Indeed, consider the Cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[*/Z(H)]\times\Bun_{H}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[*/Z(H)]\times[*/S_{H}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[*/S_{H}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times f_{c,H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{c,H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{c}}

where
m
is induced by the closed embedding
[*/Z(H)]\subset\Bun_{Z(H)}
and the natural action of
\Bun_{Z(H)}
on
\Bun_{H}
,
m_{c}
is induced by the natural map
[*/Z(H)]\to[*/S_{H}]
and the natural multiplication on
[*/S_{H}]
. By base change and Hecke eigen-property of
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}
, one has

m_{c}^{*}f_{c,H,!}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}))\cong(\mathrm{id}\times f_{c,H})_{!}m^{*}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}))\cong\underline{k}_{[*/Z(H)]}\boxtimes f_{c,H,!}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e})).

This implies that
f_{c,H,!}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}))
is constant.

Therefore, consider the map
i:*\to[*/S_{H}]
, we have

\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}))\cong i^{*}f_{c,H,!}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}))\otimes\Gamma_{c}([*/S_{H}])

in which the first factor is a perfect complex. Therefore, we have

(5.39)

\operatorname{tr}(\Frob,\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e})))=\operatorname{tr}(\Frob,i^{*}f_{c,H,!}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e})))\cdot|S_{H}(\mathbb{F}_{q})|^{-1},

which is a well-defined number.

5.5.2.
Conjectural description of cohomological correspondence

By Proposition
5.10
, to prove Conjecture
5.11
, we only need to understand the cohomological correspondence

\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{e}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{[*/S],V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}}(\Delta_{c,!}f_{c,H,!}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e})),\Delta_{c,!}f_{c,H,!}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e})))

whose general definition is given in §
5.4
. Note that there is another natural cohomological correspondence

\begin{split}\ev_{V_{H,\sigma_{H},c^{I}}^{I}}\otimes\mathrm{id}&\in\Hom^{0}(V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\otimes\Delta_{c,!}f_{c,H,!}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e})),\Delta_{c,!}f_{c,H,!}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e})))\\
&\cong\mathrm{Corr}_{[*/S],V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}}(\Delta_{c,!}f_{c,H,!}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e})),\Delta_{c,!}f_{c,H,!}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e})))\end{split}

in which
\ev_{V_{H,\sigma_{H},c^{I}}^{I}}:V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\cong V_{H,\sigma_{H},c^{I}}^{I}\otimes V_{H,\sigma_{H},c^{I}}^{I*}\to k
is the natural evaluation map. We have the following conjecture:

Conjecture 5.12
.

We have
\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{e}=\ev_{V_{H,\sigma_{H},c^{I}}^{I}}\otimes\mathrm{id}
.

Proof of Conjecture
5.11
assuming Conjecture
5.12
.

Note that

\begin{split}(\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})\circ\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e})_{c^{I}}&=\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{e})\\
&=\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\ev_{V_{H,\sigma_{H},c^{I}}^{I}}\otimes\mathrm{id})\\
&=\ev_{V_{H,\sigma_{H},c^{I}}^{I}}\otimes\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathrm{id}_{\Delta_{c,!}f_{c,H,!}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}))})\\
&=\ev_{V_{H,\sigma_{H},c^{I}}^{I}}\otimes\operatorname{tr}(\Frob,i^{*}f_{c,H,!}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e})))\cdot|S_{H}(\mathbb{F}_{q})|^{-1}\\
&=\operatorname{tr}(\Frob,\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e})))\cdot\ev_{V_{H,\sigma_{H},c^{I}}^{I}}\end{split}.

Here, the first identity is Proposition
5.10
, the second identity is Conjecture
5.12
, the fourth identity follows from
[
GV24
, Corollary 0.9]
, the last identity follows from (
5.39
). Combined with the injectivity of
(-)_{c^{I}}:\Hom^{0}(V_{\sigma}^{I},\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\to\Hom^{0}(V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I},k)
, we get

\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c})\circ\xi_{\sigma,I}^{e}=\operatorname{tr}(\Frob,\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e})))\cdot\ev_{V_{H,\sigma_{H}}^{I}}.

∎

5.5.3.
Evidence for Conjecture
5.12

In this section, we will prove Conjecture
5.12
under Assumption
5.13
, which we can verify in case
H=\GL_{n}
. This provides evidence for Conjecture
5.12
.

Recall that for each irreducible representation
V_{H}^{\prime}\in\Rep(\check{H})
and
V^{\prime}=V_{H}^{\prime}\boxtimes V_{H}^{\prime}\in\Rep(\check{G})
, we have the diagonal cohomological correspondence
\mathfrak{c}_{V_{H}^{\prime}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{G,\{1\}},\IC_{V^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C})
which gives a map

a_{\mathfrak{c}_{V_{H}^{\prime}},\sigma}:\Gamma(V_{H,\sigma_{H}}^{\prime}\otimes V_{H,c^{*}\sigma_{H}}^{\prime})\langle 2\rangle\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\to\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}.

Putting all these maps for
V_{H}^{\prime}\in\Irr(\Rep(\check{H}))
together, one obtains a map

a_{\sigma}:\bigoplus_{V_{H}^{\prime}\in\Irr(\Rep(\check{H}))}\Gamma(V_{H,\sigma_{H}}^{\prime}\otimes V_{H,c^{*}\sigma_{H}}^{\prime})\langle 2\rangle\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\to\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}.

Furthermore, one obtains an action map for the free tensor algebra

a^{\otimes}_{\sigma}:(\bigoplus_{V_{H}^{\prime}\in\Irr(\Rep(\check{H}))}\Gamma(V_{H,\sigma_{H}}^{\prime}\otimes V_{H,c^{*}\sigma_{H}}^{\prime})\langle 2\rangle)^{\otimes}\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}\to\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}.

We make the following assumption:

Assumption 5.13
.

The following statements are true:

(1)

For every
e^{\prime}\in\pi_{1}(H)
, we have
\dim H^{0}(\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e^{\prime}})=1
;

(2)

The map

H^{*}(a^{\otimes}_{\sigma})|_{H^{0}}:H^{*}((\bigoplus_{V_{H}^{\prime}\in\Irr(\Rep(\check{H}))}\Gamma(V_{H,\sigma_{H}}^{\prime}\otimes V_{H,c^{*}\sigma_{H}}^{\prime})\langle 2\rangle)^{\otimes})\otimes H^{0}(\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})\to H^{*}(\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})

is surjective.

The main result in this section is the following:

Proposition 5.14
.

Under Assumption
5.13
, Conjecture
5.12
is true; hence, Conjecture
5.11
is true.

Proof of Proposition
5.14
.

Since the functor
\Gamma_{c}:\mathrm{Shv}([*/S])_{c}\to\mathrm{Vect}
is faithful, we only need to check that

\Gamma_{c}(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{e})=\ev_{V_{H,\sigma_{H},c^{I}}^{I}}\otimes\mathrm{id}\in\Hom^{0}(V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e},\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}).

Note that
\Gamma_{c}(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{e})=a_{\mathfrak{c}_{c^{I}},\sigma}^{e}
in which the later is defined in (
5.2
), we are reduced to show

(5.40)

a_{\mathfrak{c}_{c^{I}},\sigma}^{e}=\ev_{V_{H,\sigma_{H},c^{I}}^{I}}\otimes\mathrm{id}\in\Hom^{0}(V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e},\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}).

Since
\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}=\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}))
, there is a canonical element

(5.41)

\ev_{\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}}\in H^{0}(\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e})^{*}

defined as

\ev_{\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}}:\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}\cong\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}))\to\Gamma_{c}(\omega_{\Bun_{H}^{e}})\to k.

We have the following lemma:

Lemma 5.15
.

We have
a_{\mathfrak{c}_{c^{I}},\sigma}^{e,*}\ev_{\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}}=\ev_{\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}}\otimes\ev_{V_{H,\sigma_{H},c^{I}}^{I}}\in H^{0}(\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e})^{*}\otimes V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I*}
.

Proof of Lemma
5.15
.

Note that
\ev_{\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{e}}\otimes\ev_{V_{H,\sigma_{H},c^{I}}^{I}}=\ev_{T_{V_{H}^{I},c^{I}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}}
where

\ev_{T_{V_{H}^{I},c^{I}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}}\in\Hom^{0}(T_{V_{H}^{I},c^{I}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(T_{V_{H}^{I},c^{I}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}),\omega_{\Bun_{H}})\cong\Hom^{0}(\Gamma_{c}(T_{V_{H}^{I},c^{I}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e}\otimes\mathbb{D}(T_{V_{H}^{I},c^{I}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{H}}^{e})),k)

is the natural map. The claim follows from the following fact: For any
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{H})_{c}
, evaluating
\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{c^{I}}}
on
\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{F})
gives a natural map

\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{c^{I}}}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{F})):\Gamma_{c}(T_{V_{H}^{I},c^{I}}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathbb{D}(T_{V_{H}^{I},c^{I}}\mathcal{F}))\to\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{F})).

This map satisfies
\eta_{\mathfrak{c}_{c^{I}}}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathbb{D}(\mathcal{F}))^{*}\ev_{\mathcal{F}}=\ev_{T_{V_{H}^{I},c^{I}}\mathcal{F}}
.
∎

By Lemma
5.15
, and Assumption
5.13
(1), we know

(5.42)

a_{\mathfrak{c}_{c^{I}},\sigma}|_{H^{0}}=(\ev_{V_{H,\sigma_{H},c^{I}}^{I}}\otimes\mathrm{id})|_{H^{0}}\in\Hom(V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\otimes H^{0}(\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}),H^{0}(\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})).

We have the following lemma:

Lemma 5.16
.

We have

a_{\mathfrak{c}_{c^{I}},\sigma}\circ a_{\sigma}^{\otimes}=a_{\sigma}^{\otimes}\circ a_{\mathfrak{c}_{c^{I}},\sigma}\in\Hom^{0}(V_{\sigma,c^{I}}^{I}\otimes(\bigoplus_{V_{H}^{\prime}\in\Irr(\Rep(\check{H}))}\Gamma(V_{H,\sigma_{H}}^{\prime}\otimes V_{H,c^{*}\sigma_{H}}^{\prime})\langle 2\rangle)^{\otimes}\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma},\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}).

Here the composition is understood as in §
5.1.2
.

Proof.

Note that we are in the situation of §
5.1.3
. Indeed, the cohomological correspondences involved here all come from local special cohomological correspondences. Since the local Plancherel algebra has non-negative degrees,
[
LW25
, Assumption 4.46]
is satisfied if the genus of the curve
g(C)\neq 1
. In this case, the statement is true by the discussion in §
5.1.3
. One can easily remove the condition
g(C)\neq 1
since the leg is fixed in one of the Hecke actions.
∎

Now, (
5.40
) follows from a combination of (
5.42
), Lemma
5.16
, and Assumption
5.13
(2). This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.14
under Assumption
5.13
.

∎

6.
Application: higher Rankin–Selberg integrals

In this section, we work towards a proof of Theorem
1.7
.

•

In §
6.1
, we review the construction of the
\sigma
-isotypic part in the cohomology of Shtukas for
G=\GL_{n}
.

•

In §
6.2
, we study the Rankin–Selberg cycle classes for
G=\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n-1}
.

•

In §
6.3
, we study the diagonal cycle classes for
G=\GL_{n}
.

•

In §
6.4
, we complete the proof of Theorem
1.7
.

6.1.
Isotypic part

We first define the
\sigma
-isotypic part map (
1.22
) used in the formulation of Theorem
1.7
. They will be defined using techniques of §
5.3
.

For
G=\GL_{n}
, we have
S=G/[G,G]=\mathbb{G}_{m}
. We use
\Bun_{\GL_{n}}^{d}\subset\Bun_{\GL_{n}}
to denote the connected component consisting of vector bundles of degree
d
.

We fix a geometrically irreducible Weil local system
\sigma_{n}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\GL_{n}}^{\mathrm{arith}}(k)
. There is an associated Hecke eigensheaf
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\in\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{\GL_{n}})
constructed in
[
FGV02
]
. We refer to
[
LW25
, §7.2]
for a summary of its properties and normalization (which is slightly different from the normalization in
[
FGV02
]
by a twist).

In this case, the construction in §
5.3
makes sense. Indeed, consider the map
f_{n}=\det:\Bun_{\GL_{n}}\to\Bun_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
. The following proposition verifies Assumption
5.5
.

Proposition 6.1
.

The functor

f_{n}^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}:\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})\to\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{\GL_{n}})

preserves compact objects.

The proof will be given in §
6.1.1
.

For each
d\in\mathbb{Z}
and
V^{I}\in\Rep(\GL_{n}^{I})
, the construction in §
5.3
gives a map

\xi_{\sigma_{n},I}^{d}:V_{\sigma_{n}}^{I}\to l_{I,!}(\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{\GL_{n},I}^{d}}).

In particular, taking
V^{I}=\Std_{n}^{\underline{\epsilon}}
, for each
\underline{\epsilon}\in\{\pm 1\}^{r}_{0}
, we get a map

(6.1)

\xi_{\sigma_{n},\underline{\epsilon}}=(\xi_{\sigma_{n},\underline{\epsilon}}^{d})_{d\in\mathbb{Z}}:=(\xi_{\sigma_{n},I}^{d})_{d\in\mathbb{Z}}:\sigma_{n}^{\underline{\epsilon}}\to\prod_{d\in\mathbb{Z}}l_{I,!}(\IC_{\Std_{n}^{\underline{\epsilon}}}|_{\Sht_{\GL_{n},I}^{d}}).

In our application, we consider
G=\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n-1}
. For geometrically irreducible Weil local system
\sigma=(\sigma_{n},\sigma_{n-1})\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n-1}}^{\mathrm{arith}}(k)
, we consider the functor

(6.2)

f_{n}\times f_{n-1}:\Bun_{\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n-1}}\to\Bun_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{2}}

and use the Hecke eigensheaf

\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}:=\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\boxtimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n-1}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\in\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n-1}}).

The same construction gives

(6.3)

\xi_{\sigma,\underline{\epsilon}}=(\xi_{\sigma,\underline{\epsilon}}^{(d_{n},d_{n-1})})_{(d_{n},d_{n-1})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}:(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n-1})^{\underline{\epsilon}}\to\prod_{(d_{n},d_{n-1})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}l_{I,!}(\IC_{(\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n-1})^{\underline{\epsilon}}}|_{\Sht_{\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n-1},I}^{(d_{n},d_{n-1})}}).

This is an enhancement of the map (
1.22
) which recovers the map (
1.22
) by taking global section. The injectivity of the map (
1.22
) is a consequence of Theorem
6.12
.

6.1.1.
Proof of Proposition
6.1

In this section, we prove Proposition
6.1
.
For an irreducible
\SL_{n}
-local system
\sigma_{n}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\SL_{n}}(k)
, regarding it as a
\GL_{n}
-local system, the associated Hecke eigensheaf
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}
descends to a Hecke eigensheaf
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n},\PGL_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\in\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{\PGL_{n}})
. That is, consider the map
h:\Bun_{\GL_{n}}\to\Bun_{\PGL_{n}}
, we have
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\cong h^{*}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n},\PGL_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}
.

We have the following lemma:

Lemma 6.2
.

For any irreducible
\SL_{n}
-local system
\sigma_{n}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\SL_{n}}(k)
, the corresponding Hecke eigensheaf
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n},\PGL_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\in\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{\PGL_{n}})
is compact.

Proof of Lemma
6.2
.

This is a direct consequence of
[
GR25
]
. To make our result minimally depend on characteristic zero techniques, we provide an independent proof. Consider the map
i_{\sigma_{n}}:*\to\mathrm{Loc}_{\SL_{n}}
induced by the point
\sigma_{n}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\SL_{n}}(k)
. Since
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n},\PGL_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}
agrees with
(i_{\sigma_{n}}k)*\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Whit}}
up to a cohomological shift, we only need to show that
(i_{\sigma_{n}}k)*\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Whit}}
is compact. Here

\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Whit}}\in\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{\PGL_{n}})

is the Whittaker sheaf and

*:\mathrm{QCoh}(\mathrm{Loc}_{\SL_{n}})\otimes\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{\PGL_{n}})\to\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}(\Bun_{\PGL_{n}})

is the spectral action. Since
i_{\sigma_{n}}k
is compact,
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Whit}}
is compact, and
-*-
preserves compact objects, we know that
(i_{\sigma_{n}}k)*\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Whit}}
is compact. This concludes the proof of Lemma
6.2
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.1
.

By tensoring with a rank
1
local system on
C
, we can assume
\det\sigma_{n}\cong\underline{k}_{C}
. By Theorem
4.1
, compactness in
\mathrm{Shv}
and
\mathrm{Shv}_{\mathrm{Nilp}}
are the same, and we can safely discard the singular support condition everywhere. Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\times\Bun_{\SL_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{\GL_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\times\Bun_{\PGL_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{\PGL_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{2}}

in which
m(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{E}_{n})=\mathcal{E}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{L}
. Since for any
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{\GL_{n}})
one has
m_{*}m^{*}\mathcal{F}\cong m_{!}m^{*}\mathcal{F}\cong\mathcal{F}\otimes m_{!}\underline{k}_{\Bun_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\times\Bun_{\SL_{n}}}
containing
\mathcal{F}
as a direct summand, we know that the object
\mathcal{F}
is compact whenever
m^{*}\mathcal{F}
is compact. Therefore, we only need to check that

m^{*}\circ(f_{n}^{*}(-)\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}})\cong[n]^{*}(-)\boxtimes h^{*}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n},\PGL_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}:\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})\to\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\times\Bun_{\SL_{n}})

preserves compact objects. Here
[n]:\Bun_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\to\Bun_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
is the
n
-th power map. This follows from Lemma
6.2
because both
[n]^{*}:\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})\to\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})
and
h^{*}:\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{\PGL_{n}})\to\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{\SL_{n}})
preserve compact objects.
∎

6.2.
Rankin–Selberg cycles

Now we come to study the Rankin–Selberg special cycle classes (
1.4
). In particular, we would like to study their restriction along the map (
6.3
) using tools in §
5.3.3
.

In this section, we take
G=\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n-1}
,
H=\GL_{n-1}
,
X=H\backslash G
. Define
L=\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n-1})\langle 1\rangle
, which is an odd vector space. Let
L^{\underline{\epsilon}}=L^{\epsilon_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes L^{\epsilon_{r}}
. Define
M=L\oplus L^{*}
. We have
M^{\otimes r}=\bigoplus_{\underline{\epsilon}\in\{\pm 1\}^{r}}L^{\underline{\epsilon}}
. Define
(M^{\otimes r})_{0}=\bigoplus_{\underline{\epsilon}\in\{\pm 1\}^{r}_{0}}L^{\underline{\epsilon}}\subset M^{\otimes r}
. We would like to understand the elements

(6.4)

(\pi_{\Sht,I,!}[\Sht_{\GL_{n-1},\Std_{n-1}^{\underline{\epsilon}}}^{d}])_{\sigma}=\pi_{\Sht,I,!}[\Sht_{\GL_{n-1},\Std_{n-1}^{\underline{\epsilon}}}^{d}]\circ{\xi_{\sigma,\underline{\epsilon}}}=\pi_{\Sht,I,!}[\Sht_{\GL_{n-1},\Std_{n-1}^{\underline{\epsilon}}}]\circ\xi_{\sigma,\underline{\epsilon}}^{(d,d)}\in L^{\underline{\epsilon},*}

defined in (
1.15
). Or slightly weaker, we would like to understand the element

(6.5)

((\pi_{\Sht,I,!}[\Sht_{\GL_{n-1},\Std_{n-1}^{\underline{\epsilon}}}])_{\sigma})_{\underline{\epsilon}\in\{\pm 1\}^{r}_{0}}\in(M^{\otimes r})^{*}_{0}

whose components are defined in (
1.16
).

Take the cohomological correspondence

(6.6)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n-1}^{\underline{\epsilon}}}=\pi_{\Hk,I,!}[\Hk_{\GL_{n-1},\Std_{n-1}^{\underline{\epsilon}}}/\Bun_{\GL_{n-1}}\times C^{I}]\\
\in\\
\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n-1},I},\IC_{(\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n-1})^{\underline{\epsilon}}}\langle-r\rangle}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\end{split}

in Theorem
3.2
. In this case, Assumption
2.36
is satisfied by Example
2.38
. Therefore, Theorem
3.2
gives the following:

Proposition 6.3
.

We have

\pi_{\Sht,I,!}[\Sht_{\GL_{n-1},\Std_{n-1}^{\underline{\epsilon}}}]=\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n-1}^{\underline{\epsilon}}}).

In particular, we have

(\pi_{\Sht,I,!}[\Sht_{\GL_{n-1},\Std_{n-1}^{\underline{\epsilon}}}^{d}])_{\sigma}=\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n-1}^{\underline{\epsilon}}})\circ\xi_{\sigma,\underline{\epsilon}}^{(d,d)}\in L^{\underline{\epsilon},*}.

Since Assumption
5.1
is true by Theorem
6.6
, we have the fake special cycle classes

(6.7)

z_{\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n-1}^{\underline{\epsilon}}},\sigma}^{(d,d)}\in L^{\underline{\epsilon},*}

defined in (
5.3
) (see refinement in (
5.12
)). Note that Assumption
5.5
is true by Proposition
6.1
, we can apply Proposition
5.8
and get the following:

Proposition 6.4
.

We have
\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n-1}^{\underline{\epsilon}}})\circ\xi_{\sigma,\underline{\epsilon}}^{(d,d)}=z_{\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n-1}^{\underline{\epsilon}}},\sigma}^{(d,d)}\in L^{\underline{\epsilon},*}
.

Combining Proposition
6.3
and Proposition
6.4
, we are reduced to understand the fake special cycle classes
z_{\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n-1}^{\underline{\epsilon}}},\sigma}^{(d,d)}\in L^{\underline{\epsilon},*}
. This is the subject of
[
LW25
]
, where we recollect the key results below.

Consider

z_{\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n-1}^{\underline{\epsilon}}},\sigma}=\sum_{d\in\mathbb{Z}}z_{\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n-1}^{\underline{\epsilon}}},\sigma}^{(d,d)}\in L^{\underline{\epsilon},*}

and

(6.8)

z_{\sigma,r}:=\sum_{\underline{\epsilon}\in\{\pm 1\}_{0}^{r}}z_{\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n-1}^{\underline{\epsilon}}},\sigma}\in(M^{\otimes r})_{0}^{*}\subset(M^{\otimes r})^{*}.

By the definition in (
5.3
), these elements are defined by

(6.9)

z_{\sigma,r}(m_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes m_{r})=\operatorname{tr}(a^{\otimes}(m_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes m_{r})\circ\Frob,\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma})

in which the map
a^{\otimes}
is (
6.13
).

By Theorem
6.6
, the sequence of elements
\{z_{\sigma,\bullet}\}
form a Kolyvagin system in the sense of
[
LW25
, §3]
. Note that the (possibly infinite) sum above involves only finitely many non-zero terms since
\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}=0
for all but finitely many
d
by Theorem
6.6
. This confirms the first claim in Conjecture
1.6
in this setting.

Now we recollect some key properties of this Kolyvagin system. Let
K=\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n-1}\oplus\Std_{n}^{*}\boxtimes\Std_{n-1}^{*}
. It is equipped with a natural symplectic pairing

(6.10)

\omega_{K}=\ev_{\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n-1}}-\ev_{\Std_{n}^{*}\boxtimes\Std_{n-1}^{*}}

in which
\ev_{\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n-1}}:(\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n-1})\otimes(\Std_{n}^{*}\boxtimes\Std_{n-1}^{*})\to k
is the natural evaluation map and similarly for
\ev_{\Std_{n}^{*}\boxtimes\Std_{n-1}^{*}}
. This gives rise to a symplectic pairing on the odd vector space
M
, which we denote by
\omega_{M}
. Note that symplecticity here means
\omega_{M}(m_{1},m_{2})=\omega_{M}(m_{2},m_{1})
since
M
is odd. The bilinear form
\omega_{M}
induces bilinear form
\omega_{M^{*}}
by identifying
M^{*}
with
M
using
\omega_{M}
. This induces a bilinear form
\omega_{(M^{\otimes r})^{*}}
on
(M^{\otimes r})^{*}
.

We have the following result:

Theorem 6.5
.

[
LW25
, Theorem 1.2]

We have

\omega_{(M^{\otimes r})^{*}}(z_{\sigma,r},z_{\sigma,r})=\beta_{\sigma}(\ln q)^{-r}\left(\frac{d}{ds}\right)^{r}\Big|_{s=1/2}\widetilde{L}(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}\oplus\sigma_{n}^{*}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}^{*},s)

where
\beta_{\sigma}=(-1)^{r/2}q^{-n^{2}(g-1)}\chi^{-n}_{\det\sigma_{n-1}}(\Omega)\chi^{-n+1}_{\det\sigma_{n}}(\Omega)\epsilon(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n-1})
and

\widetilde{L}(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}\oplus\sigma_{n}^{*}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}^{*},s)=q^{2n(n-1)(g-1)(s-1/2)}L(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n-1},s)L(\sigma_{n}^{*}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}^{*},s)

is the normalized
L
-function defined in (
1.18
).

6.2.1.
Geometric result

In this section, we collect all relevant geometric results relating to Rankin–Selberg integrals for the convenience of readers.
Take
\underline{\epsilon}=(1),(-1)
in (
6.6
), we have cohomological correspondences
\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n-1}}
and
\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n-1}^{*}}
. They give rise to Hecke actions on
\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{(d,d)}
defined in (
5.1
)

(6.11)

a_{\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n-1}},\sigma}:L\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{(d,d)}\to\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{(d+1,d+1)}.

(6.12)

a_{\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n-1}^{*}},\sigma}:L^{*}\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{(d,d)}\to\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{(d-1,d-1)}.

Combining these two actions, we get an action

(6.13)

a^{\otimes}:M^{\otimes}\to\End(\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}).

Let
\omega_{M,X}=(-1)^{n-1}\omega_{M}
.
18
18

18

The role of
\omega_{M}
and
\omega_{M,X}
are different here: The bilinear form
\omega_{M}
(independent of
X
) is directly related to the intersection pairing on the isotypic part. The bilinear form
\omega_{M,X}
(dependent on
X
) arises from the Poisson structure on the Plancherel algebra attached to
X
and controls the Kolyvagin system. The sign in the difference between these two forms can be traced back to
[
LW25
, Proposition 7.1]
.
Define the Clifford algebra
\operatorname{Cl}(M)
to be the quotient of the tensor algebra
M^{\otimes}
by the two-sided ideal generated by elements of the form
m_{1}\otimes m_{2}+m_{2}\otimes m_{1}-\omega_{M,X}(m_{1},m_{2})
. We have the following result:

Theorem 6.6
.

[
LW25
, Theorem 7.8]

The action map (
6.13
) factors through the Clifford algebra
\operatorname{Cl}(M)
. Moreover, we have

\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{d}\cong(\Sym^{d+n(n-1)(g-1)}L)\otimes(\mathbb{L}_{\det\sigma_{n-1}})_{\Omega^{-n/2}}\otimes(\mathbb{L}_{\det\sigma_{n}})_{\Omega^{-(n-1)/2}}\langle(n^{2}-2)(g-1)\rangle

for each
d\in\mathbb{Z}
. Here,
\mathbb{L}_{\det\sigma_{n}},\mathbb{L}_{\det\sigma_{n-1}}\in\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})
are the Hecke eigensheaves associated to the rank one local systems
\det\sigma_{n},\det\sigma_{n-1}
.

This theorem has the following corollary:

Corollary 6.7
.

We have

\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{-n(n-1)(g-1)}\cong(\mathbb{L}_{\det\sigma_{n-1}})_{\Omega^{-n/2}}\otimes(\mathbb{L}_{\det\sigma_{n}})_{\Omega^{-(n-1)/2}}\langle(n^{2}-2)(g-1)\rangle

as a one-dimensional vector space with Frobenius action. Moreover, the action map (
6.13
) induces an isomorphism

\Sym^{\bullet}L\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{-n(n-1)(g-1)}\cong\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}.

Using the language of
[
LW25
, §3.4]
, we have the following corollary:

Corollary 6.8
.

The
\operatorname{Cl}(M)
-module
\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}
is a lowest weight module with Frobenius eigenvalue on the lowest weight vector equal to
q^{-n^{2}(g-1)/2}\chi^{-n}_{\det\sigma_{n-1}}(\Omega^{1/2})\chi^{-n+1}_{\det\sigma_{n}}(\Omega^{1/2})
. Here,
\chi_{\det\sigma_{n-1}},\chi_{\det\sigma_{n}}:\Pic(\mathbb{F}_{q})\to k^{\times}
are the Hecke characters associated to local systems
\det\sigma_{n-1},\det\sigma_{n}
.

Remark 6.9
.

Dually, one has an isomorphism

\Sym^{\bullet}L^{*}\otimes\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{n(n-1)(g-1)}\cong\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}

where

\int_{X,\mathrm{Nilp}}\mathbb{L}_{\sigma}^{n(n-1)(g-1)}\cong\det(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n-1})\langle 1\rangle)\otimes(\mathbb{L}_{\det\sigma_{n-1}})_{\Omega^{-n/2}}\otimes(\mathbb{L}_{\det\sigma_{n}})_{\Omega^{-(n-1)/2}}\langle(n^{2}-2)(g-1)\rangle.

This implies that the
\operatorname{Cl}(M)
is a highest weight module with Frobenius eigenvalue on the highest weight vector equal to
q^{-n^{2}(g-1)/2}\chi^{-n}_{\det\sigma_{n-1}}(\Omega^{1/2})\chi^{-n+1}_{\det\sigma_{n}}(\Omega^{1/2})\epsilon(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n-1})

We would also like to mention a relevant result, which will be used in the study of diagonal cycle in §
6.3
.

For any
\mathcal{L}\in\Bun_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, let
\Bun_{\GL_{n}}^{\prime\mathcal{L}}=\{(\Omega^{(n-1)/2}\otimes\mathcal{L}\subset\mathcal{E})|\mathcal{E}\in\Bun_{\GL_{n}}\}
be the moduli stack of a rank
n
vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
together with an injection of coherent sheaves
\Omega^{(n-1)/2}\otimes\mathcal{L}\subset\mathcal{E}
. Consider the natural forgetful map
\rho_{n}^{\mathcal{L}}:\Bun_{\GL_{n}}^{\prime\mathcal{L}}\to\Bun_{\GL_{n}}
defined by
\rho_{n}^{\mathcal{L}}(\Omega^{(n-1)/2}\otimes\mathcal{L}\subset\mathcal{E})=\mathcal{E}
. It restricts to maps on connected components
\rho_{n}^{\mathcal{L},d}:\Bun_{\GL_{n}}^{\prime\mathcal{L},d}\to\Bun_{\GL_{n}}^{d}
.

Theorem 6.10
.

[
Lys99
]

For any geometrically irreducible local systems
\sigma_{n},\sigma_{n}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\GL_{n}}(k)
, there is a canonical isomorphism

\begin{split}\Gamma_{c}(\rho_{n}^{\mathcal{L},d,*}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}))\\
\cong\\
\Sym^{d_{\mathcal{L}}}(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{\prime})\langle 2\rangle)\langle-2(d_{\mathcal{L}}-n(g-1))\rangle\otimes(\mathbb{L}_{\det\sigma_{n}})_{\mathcal{L}}\langle-(g-1)\rangle\otimes(\mathbb{L}_{\det\sigma_{n}^{\prime}})_{\mathcal{L}}\langle-(g-1)\rangle\end{split}.

where
d_{\mathcal{L}}=d-n\deg\mathcal{L}\otimes\Omega^{(n-1)/2}
.

6.3.
Diagonal cycles

We are now going to use techniques from §
5.5
to study the diagonal cycle classes. In this section, we take
H=\GL_{n}
,
G=H\times H
, and
X=H\backslash G
. For any
V^{I}\in\Rep(\GL_{n}^{I})^{\heartsuit}
and
d\in\mathbb{Z}
, we
would like to understand the diagonal cycle

(6.14)

\langle-,-\rangle_{V^{I}}^{d}=\Delta_{\Hk,I,!}[\Sht_{\GL_{n},V^{I}}^{d}/C^{I}]\in\Hom^{0}((l_{I,!}(\IC_{V^{I}}|_{\Sht_{\GL_{n},I}^{d}}))^{\otimes 2},\underline{k}_{C^{I}}).

That is, for any geometrically irreducible Weil local system
\sigma_{n}\in\mathrm{Loc}_{\GL_{n}}^{\mathrm{arith}}(k)
, we want to understand its restriction to the
\sigma_{n}
-isotypic part

(6.15)

\begin{split}\langle-,-\rangle_{V^{I},\sigma_{n}}^{d}&=\Delta_{\Hk,I,!}[\Sht_{\GL_{n},V^{I}}^{d}/C^{I}]\circ(\xi_{\sigma_{n},I}\otimes\xi_{\sigma_{n}^{*},I})\\
&=\Delta_{\Hk,I,!}[\Sht_{\GL_{n},V^{I}}/C^{I}]\circ(\xi_{\sigma_{n},I}^{d}\otimes\xi_{\sigma_{n}^{*},I}^{d})\\
&\in\Hom^{0}(V_{\sigma_{n}}^{I}\otimes V_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{I},\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\end{split}.

Note that when
V^{I}=\Std_{n}^{\underline{\epsilon}}
, this element is (
1.10
).

For this, we take the diagonal cohomological correspondence defined in (
3.4
)

(6.16)

\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\Hk_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}\boxtimes V^{I}}}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}}).

The result of Theorem
3.3
gives us

Proposition 6.11
.

We have

\Delta_{\Hk,I,!}[\Sht_{\GL_{n},V^{I}}/C^{I}]=\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}}).

In particular, we have

\langle-,-\rangle_{V^{I},\sigma_{n}}^{d}=\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}})\circ(\xi_{\sigma_{n},I}^{d}\otimes\xi_{\sigma_{n}^{*},I}^{d}).

Since
\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\cong\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}})
, we can apply results in §
5.5
, in particular Conjecture
5.11
. By Proposition
5.14
, we only need to verify Assumption
5.13
. In our case, this is provided by Theorem
6.14
. Therefore, we obtain the following:

Theorem 6.12
.

For every
d\in\mathbb{Z}
and
V^{I}\in\Rep(\GL_{n}^{I})
, we have

(6.17)

\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c}_{V^{I}})\circ(\xi_{\sigma_{n},I}^{d}\otimes\xi_{\sigma_{n}^{*},I}^{d})=(\ln q)\cdot\Res_{s=1}L(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*},s)\cdot\ev_{V_{\sigma_{n}}^{I}}\in\Hom^{0}(V_{\sigma_{n}}^{I}\otimes V_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{I},\underline{k}_{C^{I}}).

Here
\ev_{V_{\sigma_{n}}^{I}}:V_{\sigma_{n}}^{I}\otimes V_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{I}\to\underline{k}_{C^{I}}
is the evaluation map.

The proof will be given in §
6.3.2
.

Combing Proposition
6.11
and Theorem
6.12
, we obtain

Corollary 6.13
.

For any irreducible representation
V^{I}\in\Rep(\GL_{n}^{I})^{\heartsuit}
and
d\in\mathbb{Z}
, the pairing

\langle-,-\rangle_{{V^{I}},\sigma_{n}}^{d}:V_{\sigma_{n}}^{I}\otimes V_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{I}\to\underline{k}_{C^{I}}

is non-degenerate.

In our application, we would like to take
H=\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n-1}
. In this case, for each
(d_{n},d_{n-1})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, we study the diagonal cycle

(6.18)

\begin{split}\langle-,-\rangle_{\underline{\epsilon}}^{(d_{n},d_{n-1})}=\Delta_{\Hk,I,!}[\Sht_{\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n-1},(\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n-1})^{\underline{\epsilon}}}^{(d_{n},d_{n-1})}/C^{I}]\\
\in\Hom^{0}((l_{I,!}(\IC_{(\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n-1})^{\underline{\epsilon}}}|_{\Sht_{\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n-1},I}}^{(d_{n},d_{n-1})}))^{\otimes 2},\underline{k}_{C^{I}})\end{split}

and the diagonal cohomological correspondence

\mathfrak{c}_{(\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n-1})^{\underline{\epsilon}}}

as well as the restriction of the intersection pairing to
\sigma
-isotypic part

(6.19)

\langle-,-\rangle_{\underline{\epsilon},\sigma}^{(d_{n},d_{n-1})}=\langle-,-\rangle_{\underline{\epsilon}}^{(d_{n},d_{n-1})}\circ(\xi_{\sigma,\underline{\epsilon}}\otimes\xi_{\sigma^{*},\underline{\epsilon}})\in\Hom^{0}((\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n-1})^{\underline{\epsilon}}\otimes(\sigma_{n}^{*}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}^{*})^{\underline{\epsilon}},\underline{k}_{C^{I}})

where
\sigma=(\sigma_{n},\sigma_{n-1})
and

. Then results similar to Proposition

and Theorem

\begin{split}\operatorname{tr}_{\Sht,C^{I}}(\mathfrak{c}_{(\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n-1})^{\underline{\epsilon}}})\circ(\xi_{\sigma,\underline{\epsilon}}^{(d_{n},d_{n-1})}\otimes\xi_{\sigma^{*},\underline{\epsilon}}^{(d_{n},d_{n-1})})\\
=(\ln q)^{2}\cdot\Res_{s=1}L(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*},s)\Res_{s=1}L(\sigma_{n-1}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}^{*},s)\cdot\ev_{(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n-1})^{\underline{\epsilon}}}\\
\in\Hom^{0}((\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n-1})^{\underline{\epsilon}}\otimes(\sigma_{n}^{*}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}^{*})^{\underline{\epsilon}},\underline{k}_{C^{I}}).\end{split}

6.3.1.
Geometric result

To apply Conjecture
5.11
, we need to verify Assumption
5.13
to apply Proposition
5.14
. We also need to compute the scalar
\operatorname{tr}(\Frob,\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}))
involved in Conjecture
5.11
. All of these will follow from a description of
\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV}})
which we are going to give.

Take
V=\Std_{n}\in\Rep(\GL_{n})
and the diagonal cohomological correspondence
\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n}}
, we obtain a Hecke action map introduced in §
5.1.1

(6.21)

a_{\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n},\sigma_{n}\boxtimes\sigma_{n}^{*}}:\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})\langle 2\rangle\otimes\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV}})\to\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}).

Recall we have the canonical elements

\ev_{\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}}\in H^{0}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}))^{*}

defined in (
5.41
). We also have the fundamental class
\mathrm{coev}_{\sigma_{n}}([C])\in H^{0}(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})\langle 2\rangle)
.

The following result completely describes
\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV}})
:

Theorem 6.14
.

For every
d\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have
\dim H^{0}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}))=1
, and the action map (
6.21
) induces an isomorphism of graded vector spaces

(6.22)

H^{*}(\Sym^{\bullet}(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})\langle 2\rangle))[\frac{1}{\mathrm{coev}_{\sigma_{n}}([C])}]\cdot H^{0}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}H^{*}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV}})).

Proof of Theorem
6.14
.

First note that both sides of (
6.22
) carry bi-gradings defined as follows: on the right-hand side, we set

\deg H^{i}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},e}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},e}))=(i,e).

On the left hand side, we have

\deg H^{i}(\Sym^{e}(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})\langle 2\rangle))=(i,e).

We first prove the following:

Lemma 6.15
.

Both sides of (
6.22
) have the same bi-graded dimension.

Proof of lemma.

This follows from
[
Lys01
, Main Global Theorem]
. For the convenience of readers, we reproduce the proof here.

For each
d\in\mathbb{Z}
, there exists a quasi-compact open substack
j:U\hookrightarrow\Bun_{\GL_{n}}^{d}
and an integer
e\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

•

\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}
is a clean extension from
U\subset\Bun_{\GL_{n}}^{d}
;

•

For any
\mathcal{E}\in U
and
\mathcal{L}\in\Pic
with
\deg\mathcal{L}\leq e
, we have
H^{1}(\Hom(\mathcal{L}\otimes\Omega^{(n-1)/2},\mathcal{E}))=0
.

Take
U
,
e
as above. For any
\mathcal{L}
as above, after restricting to
U
, the map
\rho_{n}^{\mathcal{L},d}
is the complementary of zero section in a smooth vector bundle of rank
H^{0}(\Hom(\mathcal{L}\otimes\Omega^{(n-1)/2},\mathcal{E}))=d_{\mathcal{L}}-n(g-1)
. Therefore, we have a fiber sequence of objects in
\mathrm{Shv}(U)
:

(6.23)

\mathcal{F}\to(\rho_{n,!}^{\mathcal{L},d}\underline{k}_{\Bun_{\GL_{n}}^{\prime\mathcal{L},d}})|_{U}\langle 2(d_{\mathcal{L}}-n(g-1))\rangle\to\underline{k}_{U}.

where
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Shv}(U)^{\leq-(d_{\mathcal{L}}-n(g-1))}
. Applying the functor
\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes j_{!}(-))
to this fiber sequence, we obtain a fiber sequence

(6.24)

\Gamma_{c}(j^{*}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d})\otimes\mathcal{F})\to\Gamma_{c}(\rho_{n}^{\mathcal{L},d,*}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}))\langle 2(d_{\mathcal{L}}-n(g-1))\rangle\to\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d})

By Theorem
6.10
, we have

\Gamma_{c}(\rho_{n}^{\mathcal{L},d,*}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}))\langle 2(d_{\mathcal{L}}-n(g-1))\rangle\cong\Sym^{d_{\mathcal{L}}}(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})\langle 2\rangle).

Therefore, the fiber sequence above is identified with

\Gamma_{c}(j^{*}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d})\otimes\mathcal{F})\to\Sym^{d_{\mathcal{L}}}(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})\langle 2\rangle)\to\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d})

Since the functor
\Gamma_{c}:\mathrm{Shv}(U)\to\mathrm{Vect}
has bounded above cohomological dimension, by taking
\deg\mathcal{L}
sufficiently small, we can make the first term arbitrarily connective. This implies that for any
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, there are isomorphisms

H^{i}(\Sym^{k}(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})\langle 2\rangle))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}H^{i}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}))

for sufficiently large
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. One easily sees that this implies that both sides of (
6.22
) have the same bi-graded dimension. This ends the proof of the lemma.
∎

In particular, we know that
\dim H^{0}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}))=1
.

When
g(C)=1
and
n>1
, such
\sigma_{n}
does not exist, and there is nothing to prove. When
g(C)=1
and
n=1
, one can verify the statement by hand. Therefore, we can assume
g(C)\neq 1
. In this case, by the proof of Lemma
5.16
, the action map (
6.21
) induces a map

(6.25)

a_{\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n}},\sigma_{n}\boxtimes\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\Sym}:H^{*}(\Sym^{\bullet}(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})\langle 2\rangle))\otimes H^{*}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}))\to H^{*}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV}})).

This equips
H^{*}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}))
with a structure of
H^{*}(\Sym^{\bullet}(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})\langle 2\rangle))
-module. We have the following lemma:

Lemma 6.16
.

The induced map

(6.26)

H^{*}(\Sym^{\bullet}(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})\langle 2\rangle))\cdot H^{0}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}))\to H^{*}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}))

is an injection.

Proof of lemma.

We claim that there exists another bi-graded action map

(6.27)

a_{\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n}^{*}\boxtimes\Std_{n}^{*}},\sigma_{n}\boxtimes\sigma_{n}^{*}}:\Gamma(\sigma_{n}^{*}\otimes\sigma_{n})\otimes\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV}})\to\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV}})

in which
\deg H^{i}(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}^{*}\otimes\sigma_{n}))=(i,-1)
such that there exists a non-zero number
\kappa\in k^{\times}
satisfying

(6.28)

a_{\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n}},\sigma_{n}\boxtimes\sigma_{n}^{*}}\circ a_{\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n}^{*}\boxtimes\Std_{n}^{*}},\sigma_{n}\boxtimes\sigma_{n}^{*}}-a_{\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n}^{*}\boxtimes\Std_{n}^{*}},\sigma_{n}\boxtimes\sigma_{n}^{*}}\circ a_{\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n}},\sigma_{n}\boxtimes\sigma_{n}^{*}}=\kappa\cdot\ev_{\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})}\otimes\mathrm{id}

as maps

\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})\langle 2\rangle\otimes\Gamma(\sigma_{n}^{*}\otimes\sigma_{n})\otimes\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV}})\to\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV}}).

Here, the map
\ev_{\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})}:\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})\langle 2\rangle\otimes\Gamma(\sigma_{n}^{*}\otimes\sigma_{n})\to k
is the evaluation map of the duality induced by cup product.

This is a particular case of the automorphic commutator relation
[
LW25
, Corollary 6.12]
. To explain this, we will freely use notations in
loc.cit
. Consider the spherical variety
X^{\prime}=\mathbb{A}^{n}\times^{\GL_{n}}(\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n})
where
\GL_{n}
acts on
\mathbb{A}^{n}
via the standard action. Note that we have a closed embedding
i:X=\GL_{n}\to X^{\prime}
given by the zero-section. This gives a functor
i^{*}:\mathrm{Shv}(LX^{\prime}/L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))\to\mathrm{Shv}(LX/L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))
as
\Sat_{G,\hbar}
-module categories. Since
i^{*}\delta_{X^{\prime}}=\delta_{X}
, this induces a map between Plancherel algebras
\mathrm{PL}_{X^{\prime},\hbar}\to\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}
as algebra objects in
\Rep(\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n})
. By
[
BFGT21
]
, there are canonical local special cohomological correspondences for
X^{\prime}

\mathfrak{c}^{X^{\prime},\mathrm{l}}_{\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n}}\in\Hom^{0}(\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n},\mathrm{PL}_{X^{\prime},\hbar})

and

\mathfrak{c}^{X^{\prime},\mathrm{l}}_{\Std_{n}^{*}\boxtimes\Std_{n}^{*}}\in\Hom^{0}(\Std_{n}^{*}\boxtimes\Std_{n}^{*}\langle-2\rangle,\mathrm{PL}_{X^{\prime},\hbar})

such that

[\mathfrak{c}^{X^{\prime},\mathrm{l}}_{\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n}},\mathfrak{c}^{X^{\prime},\mathrm{l}}_{\Std_{n}^{*}\boxtimes\Std_{n}^{*}}]=\kappa\hbar\cdot\mathfrak{c}^{X^{\prime},\mathrm{l}}_{\mathrm{triv}}

for some
\kappa\in k^{\times}
. Here
\mathfrak{c}^{X^{\prime},\mathrm{l}}_{\mathrm{triv}}\in\Hom^{0}(\mathrm{triv},\mathrm{PL}_{X^{\prime},\hbar})
is the trivial cohomological correspondence. By composing with the map
\mathrm{PL}_{X^{\prime},\hbar}\to\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}
, we get local cohomological correspondences for
X

\mathfrak{c}^{\mathrm{l}}_{\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n}}\in\Hom^{0}(\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n},\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar})

and

\mathfrak{c}^{\mathrm{l}}_{\Std_{n}^{*}\boxtimes\Std_{n}^{*}}\in\Hom^{0}(\Std_{n}^{*}\boxtimes\Std_{n}^{*}\langle-2\rangle,\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar})

such that

[\mathfrak{c}^{\mathrm{l}}_{\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n}},\mathfrak{c}^{\mathrm{l}}_{\Std_{n}^{*}\boxtimes\Std_{n}^{*}}]=\kappa\hbar\cdot\mathfrak{c}^{\mathrm{l}}_{\mathrm{triv}}.

Note that the local-to-global procedure in
[
LW25
, §4.4]
produces from
\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n}}^{\mathrm{l}}
the diagonal cohomological correspondence
\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n}\boxtimes\Std_{n}}
. Therefore, we can take
\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n}^{*}\boxtimes\Std_{n}^{*}}
to be the globalization of
\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n}^{*}\boxtimes\Std_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{l}}
, and take
a_{\mathfrak{c}_{\Std_{n}^{*}\boxtimes\Std_{n}^{*}},\sigma_{n}\boxtimes\sigma_{n}^{*}}
to be the induced Hecke action. The identity (
6.28
) follows from
[
LW25
, Corollary 6.12]
.

Now we prove the injectivity of the map in the lemma. Write

H^{0}(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})\langle 2\rangle)=k\cdot c_{0}

H^{-1}(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})\langle 2\rangle)=\bigoplus_{j}k\cdot c_{-1,j}

H^{-2}(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})\langle 2\rangle)=k\cdot c_{-2}

where
c_{0}=\mathrm{coev}_{\sigma_{n}}([C])
.
Assuming the contrary, suppose
c_{0}^{h_{0}}f(c_{-1,1},c_{-1,2},\cdots)c_{-2}^{h_{-2}}\cdot H^{0}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}))=0
for some
h_{0},h_{-2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and a polynomial
f(c_{-1,1},c_{-1,2},\cdots)\in k[c_{-1,1},c_{-1,2},\cdots]
. Since the action of
H^{i}(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}^{*}\otimes\sigma_{n}))
annihilates
H^{0}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}))
for
i=1,2
for degree reasons, we can apply the relation (
6.28
) and get
c_{0}^{h_{0}}\cdot H^{0}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}))=0
. However, Lemma
5.15
implies that
(c_{0}\cdot-)^{*}\ev_{\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}}=\ev_{\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d-1}}
for all
d\in\mathbb{Z}
. Therefore, we have
c_{0}^{h_{0}}\cdot H^{0}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}))=H^{0}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d+h_{0}}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d+h_{0}}))\neq 0
. This gives a contradiction and proves the lemma.

∎

During the proof above, one sees that

\mathrm{coev}_{\sigma_{n}}([C])\cdot-:H^{0}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}H^{0}(\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d+1}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{\mathrm{FGV},d+1})).

This implies that the map (
6.26
) naturally extends to a map (
6.22
), which has to be an isomorphism since both sides have the same bi-graded dimension. This concludes the proof of Theorem
6.14
.

∎

6.3.2.
Proof of Theorem
6.12

Proof of Theorem
6.12
.

By Theorem
6.14
, Assumption
5.13
is satisfied. Therefore, Conjecture
5.11
is true by Proposition
5.14
. Let
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{2n^{2}(g-1)+2}
be the Frobenius eigenvalues for
H^{1}(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*}))
. Again by Theorem
6.14
, we know

\begin{split}\operatorname{tr}(\Frob,\Gamma_{c}(\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}}^{d}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\sigma_{n}^{*}}^{d}))&=\operatorname{tr}(\Frob,\Sym^{\bullet}\tau^{\leq-1}(\Gamma(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*})\langle 2\rangle))\\
&=\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{2n^{2}(g-1)+2}(1-\alpha_{i}q^{-1})}{1-q^{-1}}\\
&=(\ln q)\Res_{s=1}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{2n^{2}(g-1)+2}(1-\alpha_{i}q^{-s})}{(1-q^{-s})(1-q^{1-s})}\\
&=(\ln q)\Res_{s=1}L(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*},s)\end{split}.

This concludes the proof of Theorem
6.12
by Conjecture
5.11
.

∎

6.4.
Computing intersection number

We are now ready to wrap up the proof.

Proof of Theorem
1.7
.

The first claim about the finiteness of non-zero terms has been addressed in §
6.2
. We only need to prove the identity (
1.17
). Consider the pairing

(6.29)

\langle-,-\rangle_{\sigma,r}^{(d_{n},d_{n-1})}=\sum_{\underline{\epsilon}\in\{\pm 1\}^{r}_{0}}\langle-,-\rangle_{\underline{\epsilon},\sigma}^{(d_{n},d_{n-1})}\in\Hom^{0}((M^{\otimes r})_{0}\otimes(M^{\otimes r})_{0},k).

By Proposition
6.3
and Proposition
6.4
, the desired identity (
1.17
) translates to

(6.30)

\begin{split}\langle z_{\sigma,r},z_{\sigma^{*},r}\rangle^{(d_{n},d_{n-1}),*}_{\sigma,r}\\
=\\
q^{\dim\Bun_{\GL_{n-1}}}(\ln q)^{-r-2}\frac{\left(\frac{d}{ds}\right)^{r}\Big|_{s=1/2}\widetilde{L}(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}\oplus\sigma_{n}^{*}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}^{*},s)}{\Res_{s=1}\widetilde{L}(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*},s)\Res_{s=1}\widetilde{L}(\sigma_{n-1}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}^{*},s)}\end{split}

where
z_{\sigma,r}\in(M^{\otimes r})_{0}^{*}
is introduced in (
6.8
).
We want to compare this with Theorem
6.5
. For this purpose, we first compare
z_{\sigma,r}\in(M^{\otimes r})_{0}^{*}
with
z_{\sigma^{*},r}\in(M^{\otimes r})_{0}^{*}
. By comparing Corollary
6.8
and Remark
6.9
for
\sigma
and
\sigma^{*}
, we know that

(6.31)

\begin{split}z_{\sigma^{*},r}&=\frac{q^{-n^{2}(g-1)/2}\chi^{-n}_{\det\sigma_{n-1}^{*}}(\Omega^{1/2})\chi^{-n+1}_{\det\sigma_{n}^{*}}(\Omega^{1/2})}{q^{-n^{2}(g-1)/2}\chi^{-n}_{\det\sigma_{n-1}}(\Omega^{1/2})\chi^{-n+1}_{\det\sigma_{n}}(\Omega^{1/2})\epsilon(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n-1})}z_{\sigma,r}\\
&=\chi^{n}_{\det\sigma_{n-1}}(\Omega)\chi^{n-1}_{\det\sigma_{n}}(\Omega)\epsilon(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n-1})^{-1}z_{\sigma,r}\end{split}.

Then we compare
\omega_{M^{\otimes r}}|_{(M^{\otimes r})_{0}^{\otimes 2}}
with
\langle-,-\rangle^{(d_{n},d_{n-1})}_{\sigma,r}
. By combining Proposition
6.11
and Theorem
6.12
(with identity replaced by (
6.20
)), we get

(6.32)

\begin{split}\langle-,-\rangle^{(d_{n},d_{n-1})}_{\sigma,r}\\
=\\
(-1)^{r/2}(\ln q)^{2}\cdot\Res_{s=1}L(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*},s)\Res_{s=1}L(\sigma_{n-1}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}^{*},s)\omega_{M^{\otimes r}}|_{(M^{\otimes r})_{0}^{\otimes 2}}\end{split}.

Here the sign
(-1)^{r/2}
arises from our definition of
\omega_{M}
, which can be tracked back to the negative sign in (
6.10
).
19
19

19

This is because
\omega_{M^{\otimes r}}|_{(M^{\otimes r})_{0}^{\otimes 2}}
involves
r/2
-times
\ev_{\Std_{n}\otimes\Std_{n-1}}
and
r/2
-times
-\ev_{\Std_{n}^{*}\otimes\Std_{n-1}^{*}}
By passing to the pairing on the dual space, one has

(6.33)

\begin{split}\langle-,-\rangle^{(d_{n},d_{n-1}),*}_{\sigma,r}\\
=\\
(-1)^{r/2}\frac{1}{(\ln q)^{2}\cdot\Res_{s=1}L(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*},s)\Res_{s=1}L(\sigma_{n-1}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}^{*},s)}\omega_{(M^{\otimes r})^{*}}|_{(M^{\otimes r})_{0}^{*\otimes 2}}\end{split}.

Combining Theorem
6.5
, (
6.31
), (
6.33
), we get

\begin{split}\langle z_{\sigma,r},z_{\sigma^{*},r}\rangle^{(d_{n},d_{n-1}),*}_{\sigma,r}\\
=\\
q^{-n^{2}(g-1)}(\ln q)^{-r-2}\frac{\left(\frac{d}{ds}\right)^{r}\Big|_{s=1/2}\widetilde{L}(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}\oplus\sigma_{n}^{*}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}^{*},s)}{\Res_{s=1}L(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*},s)\Res_{s=1}L(\sigma_{n-1}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}^{*},s)}\end{split}.

Then the desired equality (
6.30
) follows from the equality above and

\widetilde{L}(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*},s)=q^{n^{2}(g-1)s}L(\sigma_{n}\otimes\sigma_{n}^{*},s)

\widetilde{L}(\sigma_{n-1}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}^{*},s)=q^{(n-1)^{2}(g-1)s}L(\sigma_{n-1}\otimes\sigma_{n-1}^{*},s)

\dim\Bun_{\GL_{n-1}}=(n-1)^{2}(g-1).

This concludes the proof of Theorem
1.7
.

∎
